## Historical required implementation unit (superseded)

1. Bind an audited parent group receipt to the exact before/after frame already recorded by AcquisitionLedger. A kernel-lineage receipt alone never establishes parent symmetry. Missing ownership, changed configuration or unknown cache identity must retain ordinary evaluation.
2. Preserve the complete group through a full unobserved uniform acquisition mixture. Keep Q/P generation separate. Observed results/interventions are different typed filters and must fix their actor/target endpoints; unsupported filters decline.
3. Record general daytime/nighttime death observations at the existing common resolution/projection seam. Fix the named action/death endpoints, condition Q/P on their actual respective positive-support events, and keep action-choice mass distinct from death likelihood and normalized conditional laws. Terminal and Q-positive/P-zero cases must remain explicit. No known-role or special-cast shortcut.
4. Carry the same receipt through existing medium/seer publication production and private likelihood transfer. A publication event's likelihood comes from the configured complete-history kernel, never a role marginal. Report endpoint fixation alone does not certify that kernel.
5. At the existing representative publication entry, consume a valid inherited receipt instead of repeating private_stabilizer/full public permutation checks and request.roles replay. Count skipped checks, actual support/generation/normalization visits and ordinary fallbacks. Named detail and cold reconstruction stay ordinary and must match.

## Shared boundaries

- werewolf/acquisition_provenance.py and evaluation_branch.py: identity-bound parent/child receipt contract.
- information_set_forecast.py: canonical acquisition/physical/publication adapters and default-OFF consumption.
- action_flow.py and board_roles.py: acquisition-before-death order, real branch likelihoods and private attack-history updates.
- morning_flow.py and information_weights.py: medium/publication event provenance and Q/P mass transfer.
- board_future.py: completed recipe/cache ownership and ordinary cold evidence.

Start with the shared receipt contract and one common transition chain, not a second evaluator or more cast-specific constructors. Because ordinary cached carriers can be equal but nonidentical, a proof must be checked at its actual consumption point; metadata presence alone is insufficient. Do not perform a fresh complete comparison merely to manufacture a cache certificate.

## Bounded acceptance

Use existing completed small cases and the actual-seven finite kernel census. Compare complete Q/P histories, physical states, branch/action masses, legal-action Forecast, personal Table and cold reconstruction against independent named evaluation. Include asymmetric secret histories, observed-target splitting, Q-positive/P-zero events, stale/copy/configuration receipts, unsupported cache hits and terminal branches. Forbid full-law hash/equality and parent replay during proof consumption, while counting semantic scans that remain. Do not infer a solver speedup from fewer proof checks.

The uniform receipt path is not authorization to change original MAX selection or infer a new CO/report policy. MAX policy/value equivariance remains separately unproved.

## General generated death/report transitions (default OFF)

It adds no evaluator or cast-specific numerical transition. Generic H0 fixed-target daytime/nighttime death kernels issue TransitionReceipt only after exact canonical outcome capture and projection. Q's nighttime kernel is corpse compatibility; P's is target intervention. Receipt masses are those conditional kernel outcomes, not an unconditional probability that an actor selects this victim. Wolf-information conditioning and actor-choice likelihood are still unproved and fall back normally.

Configured uniform full medium/seer morning production and private likelihood transfer issue report-child receipts, fixing new named report endpoints and retaining full private histories. ## Typed owner information and uniform wolf choices

Following 360cf46, the same inherited-groups experiment entry additionally runs owner_information_audit. Existing SeerObserver remains the own target intervention type. Canonical filtering records complete-history event mass during generation, and AcquisitionLedger refines inherited groups by the actor and named observation endpoints. Death/report children now retain valid typed observer guarantees. Arbitrary callbacks still run ordinary acquisition without a receipt. No role marginal comparison is used as proof.

Information conditioning keeps Q unchanged and conditions P only. A typed OwnerInformationEvidence stores the semantic observation and replays from saved origin. The normal full condition_recipe search and parent roles replay are skipped only after canonical output capture and an exact owned parent receipt. Independent cold uses a separate condition_actor_state implementation with fresh engine and disabled kernel reuse. New observation filtering and physical-law projection are semantic work, not hidden full-law verification.

The existing wolf_information_groups partition supplies actual P information masses and complete conditional private laws. The policy is generated from Q conditioned on WolfInformation, never from the viewer's own scans or private law. ActorChoiceReceipt separately records Q information-event mass, P information mass, target and conditional uniform selection probability. Actual branch coefficient is P mass times selection probability. Fixed-target death likelihood and normalized conditional child laws remain separate. Only an actual canonical public-policy generation stamp authorizes a selection certificate; unknown policy-cache rows and equal-but-distinct public source aliases decline. The ordinary numeric policy calculation is retained. An audited night solve consumes the same group children and target weights; no second evaluator is added.

These counters include diagnostic/readback work; they do not establish overall solver speedup or fewer recursive actions. The full entry remains about seconds, not an initial-seven search.

Unsupported: arbitrary observer predicates, H1/GJ, scan retirement/other disclosure or strategy settings, unknown generated-policy/report aliases and MAX certificates. ## Boundary consolidation outcome: runtime route not adopted

The same-transition owner/frame checks are now consolidated before internal mint/attachment; every external consumption still validates owner/revision/configuration/fields/frame. One-shot generation scratch data is released, observation mass is folded into issued records, and dead outcome/frame indices do not retain their referents. The new one-boundary/external-every-use guard tests plus existing regressions pass 61 tests in 120.710 seconds.

A source-verified f930c44 Temp reference and rotating three-variant fresh-process batch (five repetitions each, same two cases) isolate this change from earlier machine noise. Owner checks fall 237→94 and 156→53; retained ledger map entries fall 328→251 and 240→163. Consolidated body medians remain 51.17 versus ordinary 50.44 ms and 81.13 versus 80.89 ms; peak memory and numeric state/action counts do not improve. The route is therefore **not adopted as runtime acceleration** and remains default OFF as a correctness experiment. No supported-case expansion follows this maintenance work.

A future benefit must come from consuming the checked parent group to share scalar evaluation across transport-related sibling action/information/report branches, including cases where each named child has singleton self-stabilizer. Current named caches still evaluate the same 8/31 and 6/18 value/action states; certificate bookkeeping alone cannot change that. Require a bounded state-count reduction, complete named/cold equality and end-to-end time/memory gain before adoption. Full measurement details, limitations and next acceptance gate are recorded in publication-orbit-value-checkpoint.md and shared-transition-performance-boundary-ab-results.json. H1/GJ/MAX and UI/HELP/public changes remain deferred.


## Priority clarification: the auxiliary ledger is not algorithmically required

The user's clarification supersedes treating repeated ownership/frame checks as a permanent runtime requirement. Same-group semantics and Q/P updates are necessary; a registry repeatedly proving that an internal result still belongs to its engine is not. This increment only consolidates that registry and cleans its lifetime; it does **not** yet implement a closed calculation API or eliminate the ledger. Its runtime acceleration remains unadopted.

The code already has the right foundation: GameData and BoardData in board_flow.py are frozen, and GameRuntime owns immutable common settings and creates a new runtime for a new setup. SavedCutOutGraph.data/engine and InformationSetForecast.rules/strategy plus public cache dictionaries remain replaceable in the experimental interface. The ledger's _owner_current, _groups_record, frame comparisons and issued-generation/cache-ticket maps largely compensate for that experimental openness, rather than being prerequisites of the probability algorithm.

Next structural priority is to reuse those immutable snapshots and calculation-local cache ownership. Open one calculation from a saved BoardData plus fixed strategy/view inputs; keep its engine/caches internal; create child frames only through the existing common acquisition/filter/death/report constructors. A Board edit/recalculation opens a new calculation instead of replacing the data behind an existing internal frame. The API should not separately accept arbitrary laws/groups or foreign raw cache entries and then try to certify them. Unsupported/general legacy inputs keep the ordinary entry. Once that boundary is real, owner/configuration checks belong at the external entry, and independent group invariance/full-history/cold assertions belong in development tests, not after every internally generated child.

Removable auxiliary work then includes receipt issuance/weak registries, repeated owner/frame identity checks, generation and policy stamps, cache-proof tickets, and duplicated proof-only copies of branch weights. Preserve the actual algorithms: initial lawful joint-history generation, legal-target policy, owner-only observation filtering, normalization and event/branch/choice masses, separate public Q and actual P propagation, named group splitting/transport needed by orbit evaluation, and SavedOrigin recipes for required cold readback. Root user-input validation, zero-support errors and numerical memoization remain at their actual semantic boundaries. Do not simply switch off checks on the current mutable experimental API, nor invent a second solver or a new layer of certification objects. First close ownership by construction, then measure the same bounded tasks before claiming a benefit or extending cases.


## Implemented first closed calculation scope

The agreed policy is recorded in AGENTS.md: production computes the formula; independent enumeration/equivalence/cold checks belong in tests; input validation and resource limits remain real runtime responsibilities.

`werewolf/closed_calculation.py` now opens only from immutable BoardData. The calculation captures its strategy from that snapshot and owns a non-exported ordinary InformationSetForecast engine/cache. Nodes expose semantic operations (`personal`, `action`, `reports`, `wolf_choices`, `forecast`) on their own bound state; none accepts an external law, partition, engine, cache, source frame or another node. Normal assignment cannot replace the calculation input or node data, and callers cannot construct a node. Edits create a new ClosedCalculation from the updated BoardData; old nodes continue to represent their old immutable snapshot without invalidation polling. Personal observations cannot combine different owners. This is API/lifetime ownership, not another issuance registry.

The numeric engine and action/morning/actor-information kernels are unchanged in meaning. One existing pure-kernel context is shared by saved reconstruction and closed generation (`kernel_calculation`, with the legacy evidence_reconstruction name as an alias). Within it, before_action/publication hooks do not search parent origins or reconstruct live generated Boards. Closed nodes store their generated immutable state and a semantic operation path. `export_request` builds an independent ordinary SavedOrigin/GeneratedBoard recipe with a fresh replay engine; changing that exported engine/cache cannot mutate the live calculation. Cold comparison is test-only.

No ledger/receipt/ticket is created by the new route. Ordinary/closed InformationSetForecast construction no longer imports the old provenance module or checks its cache class in the default acquire path; legacy explicit tracker support remains lazy for historical regression helpers. The selected `inherited-groups` entry now runs closed_calculation_fixture; its obsolete ledger worker was removed. Earlier ledger audits/measurements remain reference evidence and direct regression helpers, not a second selected runtime path. This is a scoped migration, not deletion of every historical prototype before it has a replacement.

Initial scope is the saved initial group-model information-fixed-v1/random-white/compatibility family, uniform configured strategy, full seer/medium disclosure and H0. History-bearing starting snapshots, MAX, H1/GJ and other modes decline at the closed entry; the unchanged ordinary application route remains available. Finite generated descendants may span multiple nights. Forecast is temporarily capped at four alive, so the current seven-player test generates a finite path and evaluates only three alive. External parsing, typed own-record/actor validation, legal targets, positive support, cancellation and nested work/time budgets are retained. No missing support is filled with uniform mass.

### Mathematical construction rather than runtime certification

At an initial group-model snapshot, permutations inside equal claim classes preserve the generated initial assignment/history law. Fix every named endpoint in public ability reports. These classes are constructed from saved inputs, never supplied separately by a caller or inferred from role marginals. Let G be this partition's permutation group. Common unobserved uniform acquisition commutes with permutations. A named action/observation E refines to the subgroup fixing its endpoints; own-role/scan conditions additionally fix the owner, known result targets and selected intervention target. Wolf information fixes its roster members and attack targets conservatively. Full histories and physical weights are still carried, rather than replaced by marginals.

Q and P remain separate. Own observation conditions P while leaving public Q unchanged. Public compatibility/role-weight projection remains the existing Model-A updater; it is not replaced with a new Bayes rule. Reporting uses the existing complete-history public generation and private likelihood transfer. For wolf information cells I, actual mass is w_I=sum(P over I), and the configured target policy q_I(t) is calculated from public Q conditioned on wolf-owned I, never the viewer's secrets. Actual choice coefficient is w_I*q_I(t); physical outcome probability is applied afterward. Thus the scalar continuation is sum_I w_I sum_t q_I(t) sum_o p(o|I,t) V(Q'_{t,o},P'_{I,t,o}). Public role weights, world-internal history mixtures and physical branch/choice coefficients are not merged.

These construction reasons replace the old runtime group/owner comparisons in this route. Tests enumerate complete named output laws and each nontrivial group generator, including private observations. The live engine does not execute those comparisons.

Root opening overhead is visible, not hidden. Process wall/import variation and tiny tasks limit generalization. Peak process working set does not improve.

Live saved-source lookup entries fall36/48→0; ordinary parent roles calls26/17→2/0 (the remaining two small calls are common fixture setup), and condition_recipe calls3/2→0. ### Final node-bound API pass

The last review removes even private `(calculation, node)` operation pairs: operations now live on the generated node and obtain their calculation/engine from that node. ClosedCalculation exposes no `_action(node)` or `_personal(node)` dispatcher. Thus a caller cannot accidentally pair a node with a different calculation through an operation API; no owner comparison or issued-node registry is needed. The calculation only constructs its root, owns its fixed snapshot/cache and provides an internal child factory/budget scope. This is structural ownership, not an added proof object.

After this change the four closed-API tests pass again (1.339 seconds); the related combined66-test pass (109.609 seconds) covers common kernels, legacy references, input/cache/export isolation and budgets. The final selected entry audit is rerun on the node-bound implementation. Pure-kernel context restoration is checked after budget exceptions.

A second complete five-pair fresh-process measurement of this final API is saved in closed-calculation-performance-results.json, with core source hashes. Final ordinary→closed body medians: **41.01→33.21 ms** (small, ratio0.810) and **74.73→74.44 ms** (seven, ratio0.996). Small ranges38.78–45.21 versus32.27–35.19 ms separate; seven70.51–81.06 versus69.97–82.91 overlap. Opening-plus-computation medians27.798→20.023 ms and33.721→31.231 ms. The earlier API-dispatch pass had effectively equal seven compute time; do not infer a general seven speedup from these tiny, setup-sensitive tasks. Initial raw measurement is retained separately as closed-calculation-performance-initial-results.json.

Final process peak medians32.563→32.246 MiB and32.863→32.641 MiB are slightly lower, while the earlier run had slightly higher closed peaks. These sub-MiB changes around a large import/process floor do not establish heap/state-memory compression. Value/action counts are unchanged8/31 and6/18 in both runs. The supported conclusion is a consistent small-task time reduction and removal of active evidence plumbing, with no state-count reduction or broad memory/initial-seven claim.

The final external-input pass also rejects invented future/missing wolf attacks: this H0 generated-path family already carries its full past bite history. The observation must use that generated history; no hidden-action mass is supplied by a caller. This is semantic input validation, not a provenance ledger. Ordinary support-zero handling and bounds still apply.


## Saved daytime Board entry: first bounded migration unit

The current closed API additionally accepts exactly one saved uniform daytime execution with unchanged strategy, claims, claim policy, seer options and full-observed disclosure. Initial Day1 public seer reports must be complete, cover every seer claim, and use the filled condition with enough seer claims. Late public log events, changed CO/policy, multiple steps, saved nights, H1/GJ and MAX still decline. This is not general saved-history support.

The constructor decodes the initial immutable snapshot and reuses its bound common action generator once. Initial claim classes retain their group until the named executed target requires splitting. The resulting root and exported cold recipe therefore preserve the canonical initial acquisition/declaration configuration. Ordinary saved decoding has tracked=None/observed_prefix=0 whereas canonical generation retains tracked=(1,) or (1,2)/prefix=1. Those fields are not overwritten to manufacture equality; complete hypotheses, rosters, Boards and histories match independently, and full canonical state matches initial generation and cold reconstruction.

The entry event probability is kept separately from normalized Q/P: uniform legal target probability times the selected physical continuation mass. It is conditional on the initial public saved snapshot, not the likelihood of its CO/report declarations. Measured examples are 2/15 and1/7. Unsupported/zero-support entries raise Pending rather than filling missing mass.

Night history is a separate next unit: after the actual-seven execute1 prefix, observed bite4 has information-cell target probabilities1/5 and1/4, total mass9/40, and changes the two-live-wolf mass from1/2 to5/9. A fixed-target intervention cannot represent that observation. Future entry must weight by w_I*q_I(target) before physical resolution, without leaking the viewer's secrets into that policy.

Evidence: closed-saved-day-results.json and three saved-day regressions cover both five/seven full canonical and independent cold states, ordinary joint laws, small-case Forecast/Table, edit/cancel/recalculate and unsupported inputs. Seven initial value search is not run. Five rotated fresh-process repetitions per mode on the same public four-alive task give ordinary/generated/saved body medians45.761/28.081/31.615ms, including engine opening/reconstruction and Forecast/Table but excluding common fixture preparation/imports. Values/actions remain10/63 in all modes. Saved-source entries fall22 to0; resolution cache33 to34 includes the replayed prefix. This removes evidence plumbing, not numeric state pruning or a demonstrated whole-seven speedup. Peaks fluctuate and no memory reduction is claimed. Source hashes and raw timings are retained in closed-saved-day-performance-results.json.


## Saved night and multiple-step entry (supersedes one-day-only scope)

The same immutable closed constructor now replays a bounded list of saved known-target day/night observations with unchanged initial public conditions and uniform H0 policy. Input history validation checks every snapshot and effective CO slot. Initial reports are still complete Day1 rows only; changed CO, new report rows, late public events, observation/deaths records, unknown bite targets, terminal-only continuation, MAX and hunters decline. A morning with any surviving seer claimant or unresolved medium report opportunity explicitly declines instead of silently omitting a due report. This is a useful saved-night/multiple-step subset, not arbitrary saved histories or full public-report migration. Forecast remains limited to four alive.

The bound node.observe(target) operation differs from node.action(target), which remains a target intervention. For a saved night, common wolf_policy reads public Q and wolf-owned roster/attack history, never a viewer's private secrets. condition_wolf_choice retains actual coefficient P(h)*q_I(target), returns its unnormalized total separately, and normalizes the actual law. Common resolve_action then acquires and resolves physical outcomes. Only the observed continuing outcome is retained. Day's constant uniform target probability is also included in the external entry_probability. Multiplying per-step event masses yields the probability of the saved continuing path, conditional on the initial public snapshot.

After the physical observation, restore_public_history_weights restores Model-A Q role weights from the existing projected public Board while retaining the observed within-world secret mixture. Public-positive/secret-zero support remains Pending; no uniform completion is added. Both numeric helpers are also reused by ordinary RootSecret saved replay. Its MAX/guardian policies are unchanged. No ledger, issued ticket, repeated owner/frame certification, new evaluator or state pruning has been introduced.

Live nodes keep their initial tracked and observed_prefix metadata through common acquisition/project/conditioning; these encode tracked acquisition owners and the prefix treated as observed declarations. They are not cosmetic fields. Exported ObservedActionEvidence starts from the initial snapshot with a separate fresh engine. Independent cold reconstruction rebuilds all operations with kernel reuse disabled, rather than accepting the live state or resetting metadata. Ordinary saved decoding still uses tracked=None/prefix0; complete hypotheses/rosters/Boards/history and public/owner Forecast/Table are compared separately from that intentionally different configuration.

Bounded evidence: five players execute1/bite2; seven players with two wolves execute2/bite1/execute3/bite4, including two nights and a live-wolf-count mixture. Every prefix matches canonical node generation, complete ordinary saved joint histories and independent cold reconstruction; nontrivial inherited group generators are checked in development only. Seven's first saved night uses target probabilities1/5 and1/4 and event mass23/100; independent enumeration of information-cell/physical outcome contributions matches. The observation's P differs from a fixed-target intervention while Model-A public role weights agree. Final path masses are2/45 and1/1750. Tests also cover public/owner outputs, past-target edits, cancellation, recalculation/cache isolation, export-engine separation, unsupported new seer/medium reports, illegal or terminal-only paths and resource limits. Full initial-seven value search is not run.

The active inherited-groups audit includes these saved histories. Use --measure --saved-history for the separate paired measurement; ordinary/generated one-day measurements remain historical evidence for their own source hashes. New evidence is in closed-saved-history-results.json, closed-saved-history-tests.json and closed-saved-history-performance-results.json. This extends accepted inputs; it does not establish fewer numeric states/actions or general seven-player acceleration.


Final saved-history validation: 58 related tests passed in59.915 seconds, including existing RootSecret replay, MAX/guardian and public-prior ownership regressions; selected inherited-groups audit also passed. Development group-generator comparisons across prefixes are3 (five) and11 (seven). No ledger/owner/frame/proof-cache method may execute during the new audit.

Five alternating fresh processes per mode/case compare the same final three-alive public Forecast/Table, including engine opening and saved reconstruction, excluding common fixture construction/imports. Five-player ordinary/closed medians17.637/8.113ms (ranges16.378-25.959/7.195-12.998); seven-player finite two-night history81.948/30.497ms (77.009-89.756/27.925-34.348). Both modes have1 value and3 actions: no state pruning. The audit's2/6 additionally includes a separate owner view, so it is not the public-only timing count. Saved-source entries1 to0; resolution cache3 to5 or7 includes replayed prefix operations. These input-replay savings do not establish general seven-player solve acceleration; the full initial-seven search was not run. Raw runs, peak working sets and source hashes are retained without a memory-reduction claim.


## Current-morning new public reports and grouped splitting

The closed API now supports complete current-morning seer/medium observations in the same uniform H0 family. Generated node.reports() still uses the existing complete morning/fake generation and Q-to-P likelihood transfer. New node.observe_reports(typed_seer_tuple, typed_medium_tuple) instead applies saved hand-report truth compatibility, using common publication_support, seer_manual.condition, condition_reports and restore_public_history_weights. These are different semantics: no fake-report sampling probability or new disclosure policy is invented for hand inputs. Actual P support mass is returned separately, Model-A public role weights remain compatibility weights, and within-world histories are retained. For saved construction entry_probability now includes these hand-support coefficients; it is not a generative probability of arbitrary CO/report declarations.

The same _report_groups refinement is used for the initial snapshot, generated publication children and observed report children. Starting from an inherited parent partition, fix named report targets conservatively, then split each parent block by its complete outgoing report meaning (kind, acquired day, published day/current-day phase, target, color, and resolved medium opportunities). Equal simultaneous reports from one original block remain one new block instead of fixing every reporter individually. Blocks are never merged across parents. Other incoming reports, named actions and own-view observations already fix or split affected members. This is an input/record construction, not a runtime comparison of complete Q/P laws or a new certificate registry.

Reason: permutations inside a refined block fix all target endpoints and transport the complete report vector to itself. The parent joint law is invariant by the initial claim-group construction or prior common transition. The existing uniform acquisition, configured fake policy, common report generation and truth-support filters commute with those permutations in their admitted scope. Thus generation and conditioning retain that subgroup. If only one of two otherwise matching reporters is also the target of another public report, it is split before grouping outgoing reports. Historical outgoing differences and publication-day differences also remain distinct. Independent full-law generator comparisons belong only to development tests.

Saved input boundary: immutable group-model information-fixed/random-white/compatibility BoardData, uniform day/night/fake policies, H0, unchanged claims/ClaimPolicy/seer options/disclosure through every history step, complete initial Day1 reports and complete same-morning new vectors. New seer acquisition day must equal publication day; medium rows must match existing current opportunities. Saved new report vectors require no nonempty public_log; late/batched/delayed declarations, per-history-step report overrides, general past condition edits/cancellation, unknown bite targets, special deaths/observations, terminal-only histories, MAX and hunters remain separate work. A new immutable calculation remains necessary for changed input; this increment does not claim general editing support. Typed bound operations reject incomplete/duplicate, delayed, already-published and owner-view report inputs. Forecast remains capped at four alive. No UI/HELP/server/public-edition changes or push/deploy.

Evidence in closed-morning-reports-results.json: five-player dual claimants retain(1,2); six-player three claimants retain(1,2,3) for equal new reports. In the six-player asymmetric vector, reports by1/2 match, but report3 targets1, so1/2 do not remain grouped. Two equal medium claimants likewise retain(2,3), then split when the new seer report targets only2. Generated public/actual probabilities, saved truth-support mass, and physical action-path coefficients are recorded separately. For the five-player equal vector generated probability is1/9 while hand truth support is1/3; multiplying one into the other would be wrong. A pre-publication own-scan view additionally preserves Q's public report probability while changing the actual P probability.

All selected generated and hand-observed nodes match independent fresh-engine cold reconstruction, complete ordinary saved joint laws/rosters/Boards/history and public/owner Forecast/Table. Metadata tracked/observed_prefix is propagated unchanged from its initial configuration (the new non-log fixtures have prefix0); it is never overwritten to match ordinary tracked=None. Nontrivial group generators, parent non-merging, timing distinction and asymmetric incoming reports are tested outside runtime. The algorithm retains complete named hypotheses and the existing scalar solver; no state pruning, whole-seven acceleration or memory reduction is claimed.


Validation of the morning-report increment: 66 related tests passed in65.602 seconds, including RootSecret replay, existing MAX/guardian, public-prior ownership and initial/separate publication regressions. Four new report tests passed again in1.097 seconds after adding explicit independent decomposition of selected-target mass and conditional physical-continuation mass. The selected inherited-groups audit passes and includes all new cases. Its older bounded scalar tasks remain8/31 and6/18 values/actions; carrying larger valid report groups has not yet pruned the numeric solver. Source hashes and exact test modules are recorded in closed-morning-reports-tests.json. The new owner-scan example has public report probability1/96 and actual probability1/24 at owner-support mass1/6; these are separately retained, not multiplied into Model-A role weights.


## First numerical representative consumer (no input expansion)

ClosedCalculation(data, grouped=True) now opts into target-orbit evaluation inside the existing InformationSetForecast recursion. The default remains the ordinary expanded consumer; normal UI factories and saved-input scope are unchanged. The selected experiment is inherited-groups --representatives, optionally --measure. This is a first numerical consumer of the already constructed partitions, not a new case evaluator or a compressed replacement for the joint probability law.

At each day decision, form legal-target intersections of the carried blocks before calling act. For an orbit O and representative r, the uniform contribution is |O|/|legal| times V(action r). No other member's action transition is generated first. At a wolf decision, retain each existing information cell I and its actual mass w_I. Refine the partition by initial-roster membership and named owned attack targets, giving the subgroup fixing I. Intersect its blocks with the configured public-Q policy support. One target r per resulting orbit receives sum_{t in O}q_I(t); the contribution remains w_I times that sum times the ordinary physical continuation value. Q's Model-A role weights, within-world secret histories, cell masses, target-policy weights and physical probabilities remain separate.

After choosing r, isolate r and leave the rest of its parent block together. Anonymous complete uniform acquisition commutes with the remaining group and does not split it. At morning, reuse existing report_children and refine each report child's partition by the common report-meaning/target operation. Newly unequal public information causes a split before its next scalar decision. The actual full named Q/P laws, physical Boards and histories still pass through the existing action/acquisition/projection/report kernels. The group is ordinary algorithm state on a nested engine-local scope restored with finally, not an issued registry, cache ticket, frame receipt or owner-authentication check. Numeric memoization retains the existing named numerical input keys and scalar law projection; different valid partitions of the same inputs compute the same scalar.

Sufficient conditions: the source Q, P, physical law and observer are invariant under the carried block permutations; roots and child operations establish that property by construction. The fixed uniform legal policies and supported own-unvisited uniform scan/fake kernels are equivariant. A personal view fixes its owner and every named result/intervention target before consumption. Wolf cells further restrict the subgroup without conditioning public policy on another viewer's secrets. A member's value can therefore be restored to every label in its orbit; Forecast and dawn scalar rows are computed for representatives and expanded by the known orbit map. Per-player Table marginals continue to come from the original complete named law. No MAX covariance or new reporting rule is assumed.

The representative engine disables legacy renamed-value keys, full canonical renaming and medium descriptor discovery; it does not discover invariance by comparing renamed full laws. Ordinary expanded evaluation retains its own existing optimizations. Tests make the old equivalent-target methods, canonical-key/public-variant/medium-descriptor discovery and ledger/proof-cache methods fail if the representative path calls them. A separate probe records uncached root actions: only1 and5 are generated from root legal labels1,2,3,5 in the six-player symmetric case. Expanded transitions for skipped2/3 are generated only in development, and their complete transported Q/P, histories, physical Boards, every outcome mass and cold replay match the representative transitions.

Bounded tasks: six players with two true seers/one fake and equal Day1 white5, execute4/bite6 then complete equal Day2 white4; evaluate four alive at Day2 day. The corresponding seven-player setup has the same cast/policy/report conditions, execute4/bite7 then equal Day2 white6, execute6; evaluate four alive at Day2 night, including the following complete acquisition/morning/three-alive continuation. Both retain claimant block(1,2,3). The latter is a finite path from a seven-player setup, never an initial-seven full value search. Asymmetric incoming-report and owner-secret variants use the same existing admitted scope.

This unit reduces target fanout and descendant scalar states before generation. Wolf information cells and complete public report vectors are still enumerated, and full joint hypotheses are not orbit-compressed. It is therefore partial numerical compression, not a claim that every grouped chance/report stage is generated in aggregate. Asymmetric singleton groups yield no reduction; owner conditions may leave only action savings. Supporting evidence is representative-target-results.json, representative-target-tests.json and representative-target-performance-results.json. The first measurement pass before disabling inherited discovery caches is preserved separately with its source hashes.


Final representative consumer evidence:70 related tests pass in115.870 seconds after disabling inherited discovery caches. Four representative regressions pass again in5.213 seconds after final formatting cleanup. Both ordinary and representative selected CLI audits pass, and timed core hashes match final source files. Complete named/cold/transport comparisons include asymmetric and owner cases. Public six-player values/actions5/22 become3/9; finite seven-player513/1549 become433/1267. Asymmetric singleton groups remain5/16 in both modes. Owner six-player5/16 becomes4/13; owner seven-player305/922 becomes305/912, so the latter saves actions without claiming fewer scalar states.

Final timing/memory uses five alternating fresh processes per mode/case. The body includes immutable opening, the same finite target/hand-report conditioning and full public Forecast/Table; common fixture/import preparation is excluded. Six-player body medians34.195ms expanded versus25.931ms representative; ranges32.844-37.450 versus23.498-39.438ms overlap. Process peak medians34,070,528 versus33,861,632 bytes differ by only about0.2MiB, so no broad small-case memory claim follows. Seven-player finite-path medians0.893746 versus0.751013 seconds; ranges0.887918-0.962355 versus0.693643-0.770294. Process peak medians42,229,760 versus40,648,704 bytes (40.3 to38.8MiB). These are bounded observed medians, not an asymptotic memory claim or a full-seven solve result. Raw repetitions and initial/final source hashes are retained. No initial-seven full forecast was attempted; inputs remain the already admitted uniform H0 family and grouped mode is explicit opt-in.


## Remaining enumeration profile and pre-construction wolf-cell aggregation

No game input family is added. The existing opt-in target consumer additionally accepts aggregate_wolf=True (requires grouped=True); inherited-groups --wolf-cells selects its audit and --measure compares expanded, target-only and target-plus-cell modes. Other modes and application defaults remain unchanged.

The development-only cProfile/tracemalloc split is in remaining-enumeration-profile.json. In the seven-player finite path, target-only evaluation has one four-alive night value state and432 three-alive day states after the next morning reports. Solver wolf partition construction is only3 cells/3 hypotheses, about1.7ms under instrumentation and about7.5KiB maximum incremental traced allocation. Public report generation is3 calls/232 vectors/544 hypothesis rows, about0.426s and250KiB maximum incremental allocation; actual likelihood transfer is8 calls/552 positive-P rows/896 hypothesis rows, about0.719s and121KiB. Thus report generation, transfer and their descendant states dominate these two enumeration costs; constructing the wolf cell objects themselves does not. Cached mornings/publication_values refer to the same report families and must not be summed as independent resident objects. Traced per-call peaks and retained-byte sums are diagnostics, not whole-process ownership attribution. The small six-player task generates no future report vectors and has only one four-alive day plus two three-alive night value states, so its cost structure differs.

A cheap upstream cut still avoids repeated report work. wolf_information_orbits reads the existing full P hypotheses once and buckets by (initial-wolf occupancy in each constructed block, complete owned attack history). Within a bucket it sums actual mass for every image, but retains hypotheses only for the chosen lowest existing initial roster mask. Only that representative conditional joint law is normalized/constructed. It does not construct every conditional cell and merge afterward, does not sample a representative world, and does not average role marginals. All other roles, scans, guards/attacks and state metadata remain in the representative joint law. All-singleton partitions use the ordinary cell constructor; the ineffective asymmetric case is not routed through aggregation.

Reason: the carried product-of-block-permutations G preserves Q, P, the physical law and the observer. Initial-roster masks with the same occupancy vector are exactly one orbit under G. Named attacks are fixed by the partition, so their complete histories must remain separate keys. For orbit C choose I*, retain P(.|I*) and compute w_C=sum_{I in C}P(I). The existing public-Q wolf policy, physical flow and future report/value kernels are equivariant; hence sum_{I in C}P(I)*V(Q,P(.|I),I) = w_C*V(Q,P(.|I*),I*). The coefficient includes the true cell multiplicity through actual masses; no uniform-completion prior or extra combinatorial weight is inserted. Q stays the existing public law, and policy is computed from Q conditioned on the representative wolf-owned information, never from another viewer's secrets. This consumer introduces no issuance ledger, identity polling or runtime permutation proof.

The seven-player root cells3 become1. Its report generator still constructs232 vectors with544 public hypothesis rows, but actual transfer calls8 become4, positive-P rows552 become328 and transferred hypothesis rows896 become576. Day3 report-dependent value states432 become288; with the root, total433 becomes289 and action states1267 become839. Likelihood-transfer diagnostic time falls about0.719 to0.454s; report-generation time remains about0.43s. Horizon/readout wolf cells are intentionally still expanded (small-case5 to5); their measured cost is small, and no horizon aggregation claim is made.

Development verification compares complete representative P and public conditional Q against every expanded cell after independent transport; orbit masses equal the sum of expanded cell masses. Tests preserve unknown medium roles and multiple possible scan histories within a cell, keep different owned attacks separate, and verify the original seven-player dead-teammate case remains two cell classes with target probabilities1/5 and1/4. Public, own-seer, own-wolf and structured selected-own-scan continuations match expanded evaluation and independent cold reconstruction in complete histories, physical Boards, Forecast and Table. A normalization-count test shows only representative conditional laws are built. Cancellation restores partition scopes; mutable-input or cross-owner shortcuts are not added.

The report-orbit census is development-only and compares full transported Q/P and both report weights. After cell aggregation its328 actual report rows fall into118 mathematical classes across the four private contexts. This is remaining opportunity, not an implemented pre-expansion generator or a claimed118-state solver. Post-generation folding is not adopted: it still allocates all public vectors and misses the requested generation/memory saving. Nor is a product of independent reporter marginals adopted: true/fake ownership, public plausibility constraints and complete scan-history correlations must remain in the common joint kernel. A future report compiler must generate representatives before constructing vector laws and keep its subgroup-dependent representation/cache separate from the public-Q reporting policy. No runtime post-hoc census or full-law proof is installed.

Measured adoption is limited to the wolf-cell consumer. Asymmetric six-player values/actions remain5/16 and aggregation is bypassed; no improvement is claimed there. In the public small case target-only3/9 becomes3/5 (states unchanged); in the public finite seven case433/1267 becomes289/839. Own-seer six-player4/13 becomes4/9, and own-seer seven-player305/912 becomes257/765. The full expanded references are5/22 and513/1549. No initial-seven full value search was attempted, and no new MAX, guardian or report-selection model is introduced.


Final validation:75 related regressions pass in81.101 seconds; the final seven cell tests pass in10.702 seconds, including two subsequently added own-wolf/original-seven structured-own-scan tests. Production code is unchanged between these runs (77 distinct tests covered across them). The --wolf-cells CLI audit passes; final profile and performance source hashes match the saved files. No acceptance-input, UI, HELP, server, MAX or guardian policy changes; no push/deploy.

Five rotated fresh processes per mode/case compare the same opening, finite prefix and public Forecast/Table, with common fixture/import setup excluded. Small six-player medians expanded/targets/cells are33.451/24.808/22.834ms; target-only versus cells ranges23.842-27.796/22.053-23.065ms. Peak medians34,119,680/34,017,280/33,972,224 bytes show only about44KiB difference from target-only, so there is no material small-case memory claim. Seven-player finite-path medians are0.890003/0.731643/0.499696s; target-only/cells ranges0.707215-0.802650/0.481204-0.555944s. Peak medians42,205,184/40,570,880/38,825,984 bytes (expanded40.3, target-only38.7, cells37.0MiB). Benefits are measured for these bounded cases, not the full initial-seven game or an asymptotic claim. The explicit cell option is retained because it reduces actual downstream states, actions, time and peak memory in the seven-player path; all-singleton/no-gain partitions bypass it.

Remaining: the public report generator still holds232 vectors/544 hypothesis rows, and forced-target named payoffs plus complete public report correlations remain on their existing kernels. Report-vector pre-generation aggregation is not implemented; the118-class development census does not change this. The next report compiler must preserve conditional joint true/fake support and public-only policy semantics, rather than substituting an independent reporter-product model or post-allocation merging.

## Pre-generation report-vector aggregation (uniform H0)

The explicit grouped calculation now accepts aggregate_reports=True; it requires grouped=True and remains opt-in. inherited-groups --report-vectors audits expanded/cell/report modes; adding --measure runs five rotated fresh processes per mode and case. Input scope, policy configuration, UI, HELP and servers are unchanged. This is a continuation optimization in the previously admitted H0 family, not full initial-seven search or a new MAX/guardian/report-selection model.

For each complete literal target set T, let H_T be the product of inherited reporter-block permutations fixing T pointwise. Reporting actors in T are singletons. For each remaining reporter block enumerate multisets of complete outgoing plans (acquisition day, publication day, literal target, color), with orbit size M=k!/product(count(plan)!). Only vectors whose target union is exactly T are emitted. Different target labels never become equivalent merely because they occupy one inherited group; the target-set decomposition is deliberately conservative. Incoming report targets refine subsequent groups. Existing medium reporting runs first and its public records refine the reporter partition before seer compilation.

Assumption: inherited groups preserve the complete public Q, actual P, physical law, observer and configured uniform kernels. The existing construction and owner refinement establish this within the admitted scope. H_T preserves report legality and complete-vector probabilities. For representative r, Q(r_i)=Q(r), P(r_i)=P(r) and continuation values agree under transport. Therefore the orbit contribution is M*P(r)*V(Q(.|r),P(.|r),r). Keep the representative's single-event normalized Q and P, and multiply only the external chance coefficient by M. Averaging orbit posterior laws would erase which named actor actually scanned which target and would change owner information; it is not performed. Full roles, acquisitions, guards, attacks and metadata remain in each retained joint law.

Compilation indexes existing full Q hypotheses by true-reporter complete plans and fake-reporter sets. Marginal plan supports only propose multiset candidates; event mass is computed by joining the original correlated truth histories with the unchanged public fake-report law. It is never a product of reporter marginals. Public plausibility remains conditioned on Q, not viewer secrets. Multiple-fake correlation remains FuturePending where the old generator has no selected joint law. Positive Q with zero P stays absent from actual report_children, never receives a uniform fill.

Each public source owns a cache of exact individual-event Q probabilities, normalized joint laws and named Board/history objects. Compact row sets depend on the observer stabilizer and are not cached as public distributions. The same event cache serves different private partitions; a named-first run can seed it, while a compact-first named read expands all individual rows with probability q rather than M*q. No private P is stored in the public event cache. No post-allocation orbit folding, runtime permutation proof, issued ledger or repeated owner/frame authentication is introduced. Ordinary generation is retained for no-symmetry cases; cancellation, work limits and exact mass checks retain separate responsibilities.

Development-only tests enumerate reporter permutations fixing literal targets and compare every expanded vector with exactly one representative, its multiplicity, exact probability and full transported law. Public 200 vectors become166 representatives; a wolf-owned partition gives188 Q representatives. Its actual P has90 positive branches, preserving Q-only alternatives. A two-reporter counterexample gives25 vectors/19 representatives while all-white6 and all-white7 stay distinct multiplicity-one events. Tests also cover asymmetric incoming targets, unknown medium roles and scan correlations, medium composition, public/own-seer/own-wolf Forecast/Table, independent cold reconstruction, named/compact cache ordering, and cancellation without ledgers. Complete-source immutability and existing action/policy/QP/group operations are reused.

The finite seven-player public continuation constructs217 distinct Q event laws (201 compiled plus16 ordinary fallback), versus232 ordinary laws in cell mode. Representative cache requests are not distinct resident laws: the compiler emits367 rows across observer contexts covering416 named rows; those counts must not be presented as217 independent chances or compared directly with unique laws. The preceding118-class census used a larger development permutation family; this conservative compiler does not claim to realize it. Scalar value states289 become246 and action states839 become714. The small six-player task has no future report generation and retains3 values/5 actions; no report optimization gain is claimed there.

Five rotated fresh-process measurements on final core hashes: seven-player finite-path expanded/cell/report medians are0.874196/0.478986/0.436476 seconds, peak medians42,266,624/38,731,776/38,158,336 bytes. Relative to cell mode, report aggregation saves about8.9% measured time and573,440 bytes (about0.55MiB) process peak in this case. Small six-player cell/report medians23.486/23.349ms, peaks34,091,008/34,058,240 bytes show no material report gain. Raw repetitions, semantic digests and core source hashes are in report-vector-performance-results.json. The semantic digests match across all three modes for each case. These bounded measurements justify keeping the explicit option; they do not establish a full initial-seven or asymptotic improvement.

Final validation: all1119 repository tests pass in876.376 seconds, including the9 new report-vector regressions. The earlier104 related tests also pass in82.070 seconds. Final production/benchmark hashes match the five-process measurement source. Structured evidence is report-vector-tests.json, report-vector-results.json and report-vector-performance-results.json. Git diff checks pass; no UI, HELP, server, policy-selection, push or deployment changes. Original untracked .artifacts/ and docs/previews/ remain outside this checkpoint.

## Initial seven-player uniform H0 entry measurement (2026-10-19)

Requested snapshot: villagers3/medium1/seer1/wolves2/H0; P1/P2 seer CO and Day1 white P4, P3 medium CO, P4-P7 latent. comparison_game changes exactly day/night/fake_reports from the retained MAX input to random; live effective policy is uniformly random with no self-bite and full configured disclosures. Historical MAX settings in archived public_log metadata are not a computed MAX value and are not used as the active continuation policy. The initial saved batch and all six complete initial joint hypotheses match the independently prepared source, with groups(1,2)/(3)/(4)/(5,6,7).

The current closed numerical API is explicitly bounded to at most four alive: _Node.forecast raises closed_forecast_four_alive_limit before solve/act for a nonterminal seven-alive root. The old seven preparation adapter is also preparation-only and explicitly refuses full search. A <=4-alive Forecast recursively evaluates the configured continuation to terminal outcomes; it is not evidence that the seven-alive entry is admitted. No guard was removed, no private engine was called as a substitute entry and no full-period seven-player value was produced.

initial_seven_entry_probe.py records the real supported entry in a separate watched process. Fixed ceilings are250,000 work,30s cooperative calculation time,35s process watchdog and an early sampled stop at384MiB private committed memory or working set, polled every20ms. These are below the existing defaults; they are not raised after failure. The sampled stop is not an OS hard allocation limit. initial-seven-uniform-entry-results.json records all raw limits, stages, input, source hashes and measurements. The successful probe reached root_forecast, then returned unsupported/closed_forecast_four_alive_limit. Work24; preparation/entry body0.027803s; whole child/supervisor0.454045s; values0/actions0; observed peak working set26,796,032 bytes (about25.6MiB), sampled peak private commit16,527,360 bytes. This is the cost of input preparation and an API refusal, not full-game solve performance or a zero win rate.

The first monitor attempt ended at its35s watchdog with no usable stage output; its untouched raw evidence is initial-seven-uniform-entry-attempt1.json. It is not attributed to the solver or treated as a memory failure. The harness originally waited for child exit before draining stdout; changing output to ordinary temporary files and adding flushed stage records avoids pipe backpressure. With identical budgets the second attempt completed normally. No production calculation file changed.

Dominant current blocker is the intentional entry scope guard, not a measured numerical hot path. Therefore this run cannot identify the full-seven solver's dominant time/memory cost. Earlier bounded continuations implicate correlated morning report generation/transfer and their descendants, but those measurements cannot be extrapolated as a full-seven profile. The next useful prerequisite is common-flow coverage and independent expanded/cold verification for all five/six-alive continuations reachable from this initial batch, preserving complete generated seer/medium reports, team-owned information, actual masses and terminal branches. Only after that would a separately authorized scope extension and same-or-lower-budget full-entry attempt be meaningful. Removing the guard alone or selecting a few saved prefixes would not establish the requested full-period semantics. No new CO/report choice law or MAX substitution is proposed.

Fresh validation after the entry probe:11 bounded/owner/cold regressions pass in8.495s, including all9 report-vector tests and the original-seven structured scan/dead-teammate tests. Source code for calculation is unchanged from4fa9d4c (all1119 tests passed there). No full-seven numerical result exists; this checkpoint reports an unsupported API entry. No push/deploy/server/UI changes.

## Formal five-alive full continuation entry

The closed Forecast now admits five alive under the existing uniform H0 constructor conditions, with self-bite excluded, complete initial declarations for current seer CO actors, and at most one living fake seer in every public-Q hypothesis. These are semantic admission checks inside the existing budget, not owner authentication or a runtime enumeration/proof ledger. Undefined multi-fake correlation and missing initial declarations return explicit Pending before scalar/action work. Six/seven alive remain blocked at this checkpoint. No evaluator or configured policy is added; the existing group-aware recursion, probability, Q/P transfer, legal actions, acquisition, reporting and group refinement are reused.

Closure argument: claims/roles are fixed in this closed saved/generated H0 flow, and players never reappear. Therefore the number of living false seer reporters cannot grow. The common fake kernel is defined for zero/one reporter. The complete initial declarations avoid inventing an initial fake declaration from a future-night noise rule. True acquisitions choose original non-self targets, prefer own-unvisited targets, then revisit legal targets if exhausted, so the admitted original roster has nonempty legal support. Night acquisition precedes casualties; only survivors publish complete obtained results. Existing configured random medium instructions run before seer publication, preserving their joint role law. Conditioning only removes/reweights whole hypotheses. Each nonterminal H0 physical phase kills one player, so alive count strictly decreases and the recursion reaches existing terminal payoffs; it is a full continuation, not a dawn truncation. The established Q/P and constructed-group invariants therefore carry by induction through all descendant phases, including owner-conditioned information and fixed target labels. Actual P-zero rows remain zero even if Q is positive.

Development-only exhaustive enumeration uses every legal day target and every actual wolf information cell/configured target, every physical outcome and every complete named report vector. It independently sums terminal village payoffs, checks each chance family sums to one, compares complete joint Q/P/physical Boards and histories against an expanded closed calculation, and reconstructs every visited source/edge independently with cache reuse disabled. Root public, complete-own-seer and complete-own-wolf Forecast (including all named target/horizon values) and Table match a fresh ordinary cold engine. This test-only enumeration is not shipped in production.

Small cast villagers3/seer1/wolf1 gives village7/15 in all three tested views. True/fake dual-seer claims retain correlated role/scan histories and also give7/15, with conditioning masses1/2 for each selected own view. The five-alive night entry from a six-player initial source gives1/4 and exercises five-night→four-day→three-night→terminal. A true/fake medium-pair source verifies complete joint medium/seer morning reporting and gives7/15. The final ten tests pass in43.391s. Individual watched jobs retain work250000/time30s/20ms memory monitoring with an early384MiB stop and a35s external watchdog. Public/own views run as separate owned calculations, never by resetting a budget inside one solve. A combined three-view development audit previously stopped at250002 work while auditing the final wolf tree; its raw attempt is retained, and its ceilings were not increased.

Raw evidence: closed-full-tree-5-{single,dual}-{public,seer,wolf}.json, closed-full-tree-5-night-public.json, closed-full-tree-5-medium-pair-public.json, closed-five-tests.json and the combined-attempt record. Scalar/action counts saved before the development tree are distinguished from counts after the enumeration. Whole job times include expanded/cold verification and are not production-only performance measurements. Six-alive support is the next step; initial-seven remains unadmitted. No push/deploy/server/UI/MAX/guardian changes.

Every incoming labelled transition and its exact weight is compared even when a source was seen before. The independent terminal payoff contributions sum to the full root value; at day roots, each contribution times the number of legal targets equals the corresponding forced-action payoff. ## Exact terminal-day cut after action/report group census

The action/report investigation identifies an existing common reduction that was not connected to the grouped continuation. With exactly three survivors and one live wolf, each day execution ends: killing the wolf yields village victory and killing either other player yields wolf parity. The selected uniform day policy therefore has scalar value1/3 in every complete role/secret-history state. The fixed CO, all previous reports, acquisitions and wolf-owner roster affect named forced-action payoffs but cannot affect this uniform average. This is a terminal-payoff identity, not an assertion that the complete conditional laws or legal future scans are interchangeable. There is no future decision at this cut.

Production adds only a narrow admission to GroupedForecast.exchangeable: day, three alive, no initial hunter and public live-wolf-count support exactly{1}. The existing exchangeable_value recurrence and existing private-hypothesis integral compute the value; no new evaluator or law representation is introduced. GroupedForecast already requires uniform day/night/fake policies. All other cases delegate unchanged to the common exchangeability predicate. Common solve support/rules/budget checks precede this admission, every morning report still passes through the existing generation, P/Q conditioning and ownership flow, and named action/Forecast/horizon/Table calculations still use the ordinary common action flow. No runtime enumeration, proof ledger or new history authentication is added. MAX and guard support are not extended.

Before the cut, the bounded original two-wolf six-night query has11711 terminal-day solve calls and34653 three-day action calls by its250004-work stop. The existing target groups are already consumed:37217 members become36745 representatives in that incomplete prefix. After the cut, the COMPLETE Forecast uses138347 work, with14288 solve calls (13645 at terminal day),2341 action requests/2226 expansions, and2565 target members reduced to2341 representatives. This is not an equal-prefix timing comparison: the former is incomplete and the latter complete. A final instrumented complete run takes13.391s with about67.7MiB peak working set; simultaneous development jobs affect wall timing. The unchanged numerical ceilings remain250000 work/30s, with the existing384MiB sampled stop and35s external watchdog. The common base evaluator is byte-identical.

Report grouping was not being ignored. In the complete census, two nontrivial compiled source kernels cover113 named vectors by77 emitted representatives, with98 cached public event laws. Report-partition requests include762 two-reporter calls without a nontrivial block and4 calls retaining the two-reporter block; these are call counts, not unique-source counts. A concrete same-public-history example has both seers initially declaring white4: the public groups contain{1,2}, but within a wolf actor information cell they split into{1} and{2}. A swap then changes membership of the actor's known initial wolf roster, hence changes the actual conditional law. Reusing the public reporter group here would mix a true seer and a known wolf/fake. Subsequent named report targets and different outgoing histories further refine existing groups through the common generator. The remaining12316 fallback public vectors are retained. These are different complete observations; equal roles or equal terminal scalar alone does not authorize merging their Q/P laws. The earlier concrete scan-history counterexample remains relevant before the terminal cut: identical current public role laws can have future scan candidates{4,5,6} versus{2,4,5}.

Further report integration at a terminal uniform average may be mathematically possible, but skipping generation also needs to preserve undefined fake-kernel/zero-support refusals and full observation/replay contracts. This checkpoint does not establish that all remaining report vectors are irreducible; it deliberately leaves their common generation intact after resolving the measured work blocker with the smaller exact change. Before terminality, deleting visited targets, report colors or own wolf membership has no established equivalence and is not implemented.

Development verification uses explicit named physical action_outcomes at each terminal cut, with no count recurrence or symmetry classes, and fresh independent source reconstruction. The original public and selected complete-own-seer Forecasts are checked by independently bounded named-target jobs plus actual-P wolf-roster partition scalar jobs. The source joint mixtures, including owner observer, must equal the original inputs; public Model-A weights are never substituted for actual partition masses. Public P has six roster cells of mass1/6; selected true P1 seer P has three of mass1/3. The owner observer is retained in the seer scalar partitions, rather than replacing a seer view by a wolf view. A selected own-wolf full Forecast/Table also matches an unsharded cold independent enumeration. The public/selected-seer scalar is2/15, every legal named column2/15, and both by-dawn victory masses0. The seer knows player2 is a wolf, so target2 is illegal and absent from its table. An exploratory reference target2 refusal is preserved and not treated as a missing legal column.

Unsharded public and selected-seer development references still stop at the work ceiling; the successful bounded shards are separate development integrals, never production budget resets. Original grouped public, selected own-seer and selected own-wolf full Forecast/Table requests finish within the original limits. Regression verification comprises13 distinct methods in two runs (10 in26.758s and3 in84.128s), including independent complete terminal trees for single/dual true-fake claims and owners, multi-true-seer publication laws, medium reports, mixed live-wolf counts, multiple nights, undefined correlations, initial declarations, budgets and entry boundaries. The final three narrow-cut unit tests pass again in0.087s after extending the negative count boundary. Initial test-only defaults/API-field assumptions were corrected; no probability formula changed in response to those errors. No fresh all-repository run is claimed.

Evidence and exact comparisons are consolidated in benchmarks/player_match_groups/terminal-day-analysis.json, with raw bounded profiles/references and the development terminal_day_profile.py/terminal_day_reference.py fixtures. Original checkpoint records overwritten by regression runners are restored after their new records are copied losslessly into that evidence.
