Stop here rather than layering speculative caches. This does not prove that the remaining states are mathematically irreducible. The next substantive option is to prove a shared scalar integral that contracts a normalized terminal report family before constructing all conditional posterior rows, while preserving undefined fake-kernel/zero-support refusals and every ordinary history/Table path. A broader uniform-H0 count recurrence would require the same proof that information-dependent report generation is defined/normalized and cannot affect the selected physical policy, not merely equal current role marginals. Until that proof and independent reconstruction/enumeration exist, no report color, visited-target history, medium identity or owner roster is erased. A development-only bounded partition can verify separate branches, but is not a production budget reset or a completed seven-root result. No public push/deploy, server/UI/HELP or shared-setting change is made.


## Report-integral mathematical design (development only)

This checkpoint changes no production module, policy, API, limit or admission. It derives exact scalar contractions from the existing equations, exercises them against independent finite enumeration and current common kernels, and leaves implementation for a separate reviewed change. In particular, neither a new report strategy nor a completed production seven-player Forecast/Table is introduced.

### Existing equation and the three different measures

Start AFTER the common acquisition, owner-observation validation and physical/public continuation projection, at a pre-publication morning. Let h contain complete roles/acquisitions/guard/attack records, Q(h) be the current public joint law, and P(u) the separate normalized actual/viewer law. The projection pi(h)=(roles, acquisitions) is exactly the identity used by information_weights.observer_report_weights; the viewer's guard/attack metadata is preserved, not required to be identical to a public history. Write Qpi(z)=sum_{pi(h)=z} Q(h). This Q is the already established Model-A public continuation law, not a posterior obtained by replacing it with P or with a physical branch mass.

Let m be a complete medium-report vector. The existing uniform fake-medium instruction law and truthful medium reports define L_m(roles), with sum_m L_m(roles)=1. Its public probability and conditional history law are

\[
 q_m=\sum_h Q(h)L_m(roles_h),\qquad
 Q_m(h)=Q(h)L_m(roles_h)/q_m.
\]

These are the existing report_actions/random_report_outcomes and project_private operations: relative within-role acquisition histories survive the role projection. Only positive-q_m families are generated. No medium opportunities means one identity family.

Given Q_m, let f(h) be the living fake-seer claimant set, tau(h) the deterministic COMPLETE newly publishable truthful vector, and beta an approved fake plan. For zero fake claimants the plan is empty with probability1. More than one fake seer remains undefined. For one fake p, B_p(beta) is the current fake_backlog_options product law: every unpublished slot uses the same own PUBLIC past, original nonself target domain, unvisited-first fallback and uniform colors. This is not an invented sequential updating rule for unpublished fake slots.

For the configured noncontradictory/uniform policy, let T_p(Q_m) be the SET of distinct pending own report signatures in positive-Q_m hypotheses where p is true. If p has no possible true world, keep the full old noise law. Otherwise

\[
 A_p=B_p\cap T_p(Q_m),\qquad
 Z_p(Q_m)=\sum_{\beta\in A_p}B_p(\beta),\qquad
 F_p(\beta;Q_m)=B_p(\beta)1_{\beta\in A_p}/Z_p(Q_m).
\]

Here intersection means plan support, not multiplication of measures. Z=0 is the existing no_noncontradictory_fake_report refusal. Duplicate true signatures and their public probabilities do NOT multiply fake weights: plausibility is existence, not imitation of the true seer's posterior distribution. For the separately configured plain uniform policy, A is the whole base support and Z=1. A shortcut must retain the selected policy rather than silently switching between them.

Let S_y(h;Q_m) sum F_{f(h)} over plans whose union with tau(h) is the complete named seer vector y. The joint observation kernel is K_{m,y}(h)=L_m(roles_h) S_y(h;Q_m). Since every selected factor law is defined and normalized, sum_{m,y} K_{m,y}(h)=1 for each positive-Q hypothesis. This is conditional independence of the approved fake-plan factors within a world, NOT independence of different reporters' public marginals. Multiple true reports remain correlated through the same h.

For e=(m,y), the current generator constructs q_e=sum_h Q(h)K_e(h), Q_e(h)=Q(h)K_e(h)/q_e. The current transfer to P is exactly

\[
 \widetilde P_e(u)=P(u)\frac{q_e Q_e^\pi(\pi u)}{Q^\pi(\pi u)},\quad
 p_e=\sum_u\widetilde P_e(u),\quad
 P_e=\widetilde P_e/p_e.
\]

The ordinary scalar continuation is sum_e p_e V(B_e,Q_e,past+e,P_e,observer). A physical incoming branch probability remains outside this expression. Public q_e, actual p_e and a forced-target Model-A count prior must never be substituted for one another.

### Exact contraction conditions

Assume a COMPLETE unobserved report family, normalized P, P absolutely continuous with respect to Q on pi, defined normalized kernels in EVERY positive-Q_m family, and a continuation affine in P whose per-state payoff g(u) does not depend on e, Q_e, an e-dependent legal-action set or an e-dependent chosen policy. Then

\[
 \sum_e p_e\sum_u P_e(u)g(u)
 =\sum_u P(u)g(u)\sum_e\frac{q_e Q_e^\pi(\pi u)}{Q^\pi(\pi u)}
 =\sum_u P(u)g(u).
\]

This is just exchanging two finite sums and cancelling the CURRENT normalizers. It does not claim Q_e or P_e are equal across observations. It also does not permit taking max outside the observation integral. If the event is already selected/observed, its likelihood and conditional law remain required; an average over possible events cannot replace an observed Table.

The safest immediate candidate (A) is the already proved uniform day, three survivors, one live wolf, no hunter boundary. Every execution ends; its uniform scalar is the existing count recurrence value c=1/3 in every supported world and every private condition. Reports do not change those physical facts. Contract the FULL unobserved morning report family to c, AFTER the common acquisition/observer/physical checks. Do not move this cut before acquiring a mandatory own result. Do not use it for a named observed report, a conditional Table, MAX, guardian continuations, future physical transitions or a partial report family. Fixed named last-execution payoffs are also affine before an unobserved family, but this is not a replacement for the existing observed named-action rows.

Positive-Q families must be validated even if their p_e or their medium-level P mass is zero. If P has unsupported pi entries, the current incomplete-likelihood refusal remains necessary. Undefined initial future declarations, multiple fake-seer correlation and zero normalizers are not repaired with uniform mass. These are ordinary input/model-domain responsibilities, not runtime proof tickets.

### Reuse of the existing common index, without posterior construction

The current compile_publications already constructs a truth-plan index over complete source hypotheses and the selected fake-plan probabilities before event()/branches()/representatives() constructs ANY conditional event law. Candidate A can reuse this common probability representation at the terminal scalar boundary, regardless of whether reporter permutation grouping is available. It need not introduce a case-specific seer evaluator or a new report formula.

The development compiled_mass_probe does the following: enumerate the existing positive-Q medium outcomes, use the existing role/history projection, compile every such seer kernel (including zero-P medium families), and transfer the medium-only masses with the existing push operation. Because every conditional seer row sums to1, the integrated scalar is c times the sum of actual medium masses. The probe never calls event, branches or representatives and builds zero conditional seer posteriors. Ordinary report/history/Table requests would still use their complete existing generation and reconstruction.

Coverage scans and assertions in this PROBE are development checks, not instructions to add them to production. A later minimal production change would share the same common kernel/normalizer definition with ordinary generation and consume its scalar integral. Actual factor normalizers/support failures and cancellation/work/time polling remain necessary; full-history swaps, repeated owner/frame authentication, issued ledgers and proof certificates do not. Inputs and any useful probability index remain immutable/calculation-owned. The independent checker/oracle in this checkpoint must not be copied into production as another evaluator.

### Small-board derivations and counterexamples

All following sources come from common generated actions and independent cold reconstruction, unless explicitly marked as raw/out-of-scope negative inputs. Complete Q probabilities/posteriors and P probabilities/posteriors are compared, including Q-positive/P-zero rows.

| Five-player initial source; first execution/night bite | Pre-report H | Medium families | Full h/fake emissions | Public vectors | Compact seer event laws built |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| One true seer; execute3/bite5 | 6 | 1 | 6 | 5 | 0 |
| True/fake dual seer; execute3/bite5 | 6 | 1 | 18 | 9 | 0 |
| Two true seers plus one fake; execute4/bite5 | 27 | 1 | 135 | 109 | 0 |
| One seer plus true/fake medium pair; execute4/bite5 | 6 | 3 | 12 | 11 | 0 |

Each morning has three survivors/one wolf. Independent named last-execution enumeration and candidate A both give1/3 in public, complete-own-seer and wolf views (12 cases). In the three-claim case the current common index retains27 source hypotheses and15 fake plans across three fake families, not135 emitted hypothesis copies and109 conditional seer laws. The own-seer view has only15 positive-P vectors while public Q still has109; the wolf view has45. Pruning the Q model down to those P rows is not the validation theorem.

* Observed Table / MAX counterexample: in the single-seer example, wolf2 and wolf4 each have prior probability1/2. A Day2 black report on2 occurs with probability1/6 and makes the named execution2 value1, versus the prior column1/2. Unobserved fixed-column averaging gives1/2 as it should. Choosing the best execution AFTER seeing a report gives5/6; choosing it before gives1/2. Thus moving max through the integral, or replacing a conditional Table by its average, changes the defined problem. No MAX policy is added or changed here.
* Joint-marginal counterexample: in the two-true/one-fake example, each of 1:black2, 2:black3, 3:black1 has marginal probability8/45. Their product is512/91125, but the simultaneous three-report cycle has joint probability0: two truthful black reports cannot require different sole wolves. Independent reporter marginals would invent an impossible observation.
* Existence is not probability weighting: in that same source, fake1's approved plan white5 has probability1/5. Conditional on1 being true, the matching true signature has probability1/3. Weighting a fake option by that hypothetical true probability would select a different strategy. The deduplicated normalizer is5/6, not a count or weighted sum of all repeated supporting histories.
* Medium-before-seer counterexample: use the specified seven-player initial source, execute2 and force bite4. The five-survivor pre-report source has18 hypotheses, two medium outcomes,45 emitted h/fake pairs and18 public vectors. Before conditioning on the medium, fake1's base10 plans are filtered to8 (Z=4/5). A white medium report on executed2 eliminates true1 worlds, making1 a known fake; its existing noise law then has all10 plans (Z=1). The joint event medium:white2 plus seer1:white2 has probability1/20, which the pre-medium filtered plan set would incorrectly remove. An owner-seer condition can give this entire positive-Q medium family zero P; it still cannot be omitted from kernel validation. This five-survivor example is NOT admitted to the constant-terminal contraction.
* Multiple-night witness: from the seven source, execute4, bite3, select both Day2 seers' white3 reports, execute5 and retain the continuing branch, then bite7. The final three-survivor source has8 hypotheses,32 h/fake emissions and16 vectors. All three tested views agree on the full joint laws and1/3 integral. Each fake still has one pending slot and Z=1/2; the prior results were retained, not reconstructed as fresh unvisited history.
* Support negatives: positive-Q multiple-fake families still fail even if P selects only a supported one-fake world. An unsupported private pi, an incomplete event family and an incoherent raw supplied past retain their old refusals. A genuine Day1 source still cannot be treated as the defined future declaration law.
* Raw report-law boundary: an own-acquisition/public-prefix-consistent raw object outside this route uses four players, recorded guards/attacks and three delayed unpublished slots. Its repeated rosters are NOT asserted to form a legal sequence of daytime eliminations; it is not a generated physical game. True1 initially reports white4, then scans2:black,3:white,4:white after exhausting unvisited targets (and symmetrically true2 in the other world). The current fake backlog uses the unchanged public past white4 for ALL pending slots, so it still excludes target4 while an actual true pending signature includes4. The noncontradictory normalizer is0 and the existing kernel refuses it; the separately selected plain uniform law is defined. This is an out-of-scope negative report-law input, NOT new guardian/delayed-report support. Silently falling back to uniform or changing how pending fake slots update history would be a strategy/specification change and is not implemented.

For the current full-disclosure/fixed-claim scope, there is a constructive positivity witness: every previous result of a surviving true claimant is published, so its private visited set and its own public reported-target set agree. At a new morning there is just one pending slot. The true unvisited-first/fallback domain therefore equals the fake plan's domain, and the actual truthful color has positive fake base mass. Any possible true reporter supplies a signature in the intersection, hence Z>0; a known fake has the normalized original noise law. Medium conditioning removes worlds but does not invalidate the coherent histories of the remaining true witnesses. The simple witness uses the current coherent generated full-disclosure invariant. It does not assert that every causally valid delayed game fails; such a larger domain needs its own support argument. Normalized dataclasses alone, including the raw repeated-roster counterexample, do not establish future-kernel definedness.

### Complexity and a broader conditional theorem

Let H_m be supported histories after medium m, A the per-history record length, F_{m,f} the number of selected fake plans, E=sum_m sum_h F_{m,f(h)} raw h/plan emissions, and R the number of complete observation vectors. Ordinary future_publications performs the H*A scan, constructs E entries, aggregates/normalizes R posterior states and creates corresponding Boards/private conditionals. Its state/output cost is at least proportional to E+R (with record/normalization costs in addition).

Candidate A using the CURRENT common compiler retains source truth indices and fake-plan maps with approximate cost sum_m[H_m*A + sum_f F_{m,f}], plus medium projection/P transfer; it does not pay H_m*F_{m,f} for seer events or construct R conditional seer states. The generic factor-normalizer study can compute Z by visiting distinct feasible truth signatures instead of a Cartesian fake product: for k pending slots, test membership and multiply slot weights, summing once per distinct signature. If no true world exists, each factor is already normalized. This gives a possible bound involving H*A + sum_f |T_f|*k rather than |B_f|=product of slot cardinalities. In the CURRENT scope k<=1, so this generic backlog improvement is secondary; avoiding h/plan cross-products and posterior rows is the main opportunity. Medium outcome enumeration/conditioning remains: a general theorem does not erase an observation that changes a later kernel's domain.

The last measured seven prefix after the scalar key spends160327+43685+17426=221438 counted work (about88.58%) in publication construction, plausible support and backlog. This is NOT a claim that all that work disappears: the common source/normalizer/index cost still exists. This checkpoint measures only small development constructions and exact operation cardinalities, not an optimized production speedup.

A broader scalar candidate (B) also follows algebraically under stronger CLOSED-FUTURE conditions: H0, no self-bite, uniform current and future physical policies, fixed complete declarations/full disclosure, approved defined normalized future ability/report laws, coherent generated Q/P support, and no prospective observer condition that can reject/reweight a future acquisition. Physical count transitions then ignore report identity. For the existing count recurrence v, terminal values are v(n,0)=1 and v(n,w)=0 at parity; otherwise

\[
 v_D(n,w)=\frac wn v_N(n-1,w-1)+\frac{n-w}{n}v_N(n-1,w),\qquad
 v_N(n,w)=v_D(n-1,w).
\]

Finite backward induction integrates each normalized report family and yields sum_u P(u)v_phase(n,w_u). More precisely, the induction statement ranges over coherent generated frames, not role marginals alone. Physical H0 actions strictly reduce survivor count; a pending-report step consumes its pending observation without another physical action, giving a finite well-founded phase order. The terminal case is the existing winner rule. For a daytime world, averaging the uniform legal targets kills a wolf with exactly w/n probability, regardless of its identity. For an actual wolf-owned night cell, every legal nonself bite kills one nonwolf, so every target has the same next count (n-1,w); the approved policy probabilities sum to1. Incoming physical masses and posterior normalizers cancel by the same finite conditional-expectation identity. At a reporting boundary, applying the lemma to each indicator 1_{w_u=w} gives sum_e p_e P_e(w)=P(w), although each individual posterior count law may differ. The coherent/full-disclosure witness above establishes report definedness, while ordinary acquisition and physical generators preserve normalized support. This is the EXISTING exchangeable_value formula, not a new case evaluator. Initial asymmetry of wolf positions is harmless for uniform day scalar averages, but named actions still require the actual joint count/target information:

\[
 A_t^D=\sum_u P(u)[1_{t\in W_u}v_N(n-1,w_u-1)+1_{t\notin W_u}v_N(n-1,w_u)].
\]

For a LEGAL forced night target, the existing within-live-count intervention preserves P's count weights, so A_t^N=sum_w P(w)v_D(n-1,w). Legal targets are still produced by the common legal-action operation; illegal own-wolf targets are not inserted. Actual night target CONTRIBUTIONS remain sum_I P(I)policy_I(t) v_D(n-1,w_I), not equal weights over public columns.

Small independent verification of B enumerates all first-day targets, actual roster-owned night choices, named physical outcomes, the independently enumerated complete report law and each final execution. It never calls solve or the count recurrence while producing the reference. Four five-player claim configurations times public/seer/wolf views (12 COMPLETE integrals) match the existing recurrence, every named forced-action value and the current complete Forecast:7/15 throughout. A separate five-night mixed-count source has P(w=1)=1/3, P(w=2)=2/3, scalar1/12 and a selected two-live-wolf owner's scalar0. Actual target contributions1/96 versus1/48 are retained; they are not1/5 of the forced-column value. Unknown count weights must not be replaced by a uniform count prior.

B is not a blanket predicate for GroupedForecast. A prospective SeerObserver requesting slot2's already-visited initial target3 is accepted as future information at the current source but the mandatory next acquisition has zero support; the current full continuation refuses with seer_view_scan_outside_public_support. A count-only immediate return would incorrectly return a number. Current-result acquisition/observer checks must precede A, and broader B must conservatively exclude prospective interventions or preserve their full domain checks. Small-entry inputs missing initial declarations and other selected ability policies likewise need the ordinary existing path. No internal API acceptance, cache ticket or runtime full-history matching is proposed to bypass this requirement.

For the specified initial-seven PUBLIC input with retrospective views, B predicts v_D(7,2)=8/35, columns1/2=3/10,3/4=2/15,5/6/7=11/45 under its stated future-closure hypotheses. These are design-derived references, NOT a completed production seven-player request or a synthesized Table. The native seven request remains at its recorded250001-work stop. No production integration, universal performance claim, new MAX/guardian/delayed-report rule or approximate value is made.

### Next implementation boundary and evidence

A is the conservative first integration candidate: a shared complete-report-kernel MASS/normalizer operation at the already physically resolved terminal morning, using the current compiler/probability helpers and retaining the full generation path for observed reports/history/Table. B is a separate larger admission/closure change; it must be gated by immutable validated input semantics, normalizer/support closure and retrospective observer rules, with ordinary fallback for uncovered inputs. Neither needs a history-permutation test or a guarantee ledger in production. Any new delayed-report or multiple-fake correlation rule would require an explicit specification and remains a proposal rather than an automatic repair.

The development report_integral_design.py contains an explicit Cartesian reference with its OWN normalization/filter calculation, a separate distinct-signature factor checker, the current-common-index probe, independent full five-player terminal sums, and conditional count references. The oracle matches every full Q/P branch and fresh independent cold source, not just final scalar coincidences. Tests cover normalization, zero support, medium ordering, correlations, observed/MAX noncommutation, mixed-count actual policy masses, retrospective/prospective observation and multiple nights. All production hashes remain unchanged. Raw bounded results, test output, source hashes and the frozen last actual seven stop are retained in report-integral-design-results.json. Bounds remain250000 work/30s/384MiB sampled memory/35s watchdog; no push/deploy, server, UI/HELP or shared-setting change is included.


Bounded validation outcome for this mathematical design: the study verifies12 terminal-morning public/owner joint cases and12 complete five-player named physical/report/terminal integrals in1.381727s,25355 counted development/common work, peak working set37,040,128 bytes. The separate25-test watched job (14 new mathematical-design methods plus11 existing boundary/history methods) passes in2.591859s,38523 counted work, peak working set41,463,808 bytes. Both use the original250000-work/30s/384MiB sampled/35s-watchdog ceilings and finish without supervisor termination. These are development verification costs, not production performance benchmarks. The full seven native API stop is unchanged; only a conditional theorem reference for its value/columns has been added. No all-repository rerun is claimed.


## Opt-in integration of the unobserved terminal report integral

Candidate A above is now implemented ONLY in GroupedForecast's existing aggregate_reports opt-in scalar continuation. Candidate B (a broad count-only root/continuation shortcut) is NOT implemented. The measured seven-player value below is now an actual complete ClosedCalculation Node.forecast() result, rather than only the previous theorem prediction. This does not enable a new UI/HTTP path, change settings or claim every eligible seven-player input completes.

GroupedPolicy.morning_value first asks for a terminal_report_value. It is available only for the already established day/three-survivor/one-live-wolf/no-hunter predicate, full seer+medium disclosure, unretired complete histories, aggregate_reports enabled and no legacy acquisition-provenance tracing. Other cases retain the ordinary report_children/solve path. The grouped constructor still requires uniform day/night/fake policies. MAX, guardians, seven-night, eight-player, self-bite admission and the ordinary evaluator are not extended or replaced.

The helper is entered AFTER the existing acquisition, prospective owner scan validation and physical/public outcome projection. It uses existing report_actions/random_report_outcomes, existing public history projection, the SAME compile_publications constructor and existing push for medium-only Q/P transfer. It compiles EVERY positive-Q medium family before P transfer, including zero-P families. Existing undefined-correlation/initial-law/zero-normalizer errors therefore remain; private unsupported report likelihood still uses the existing refusal. The common per-kernel fake normalizers and deterministic truth index establish unit seer row sums without event construction. The result is the actual medium-mass integral of the existing exchangeable_value(3,1,day) constant. A zero-work tick keeps cancellation/deadline/exhausted-budget checks on cached calls.

Existing grouped publication-kernel construction is factored into publication_kernel and reused by both the old report branches and the new scalar operation. Kernels/cache keys remain public-only and calculation-owned, with no viewer secret entering a policy. There are no added proof ledgers, tickets, full-history swaps, issued owner/frame authentication or development coverage scans in production. The ordinary information_set_forecast, morning_publications and information_weights modules are byte-identical. Full conditional reports/history/Table still go through the ordinary generation and reconstruction; requesting them after scalar compilation builds their event laws on demand. No policy or report correlation is changed.

New focused checks compare12 complete public/seer/wolf report laws against the independent Cartesian oracle while forbidding any event constructor, report_children call or posterior solve in the scalar path. Warming the scalar index then requesting all109 observed report rows and their named Forecast/Table values preserves the exact Q/P distributions and conditional action payoffs. Other checks cover disabled opt-in, disclosure/history-retirement/provenance fallback, public undefined worlds with zero P, unsupported private likelihood, cancellation, deadline and exhausted work, and valid/invalid prospective own scans. Invalid future scans still fail during the common acquisition before the integral can run. No new validation ledger is used to obtain these guarantees.

## 2026-10-19 DEFAULT MAX: development decision-order boundary

This checkpoint changes development math/tests only. Production remains at the
uniform H0 admission boundary of09ad0ed. No complete current seven-player MAX
run, larger limit, new policy, UI/server operation or deployment is claimed.

### Exact terminal reduction and its assumptions

At three living players, exactly one living wolf, day execution and H0, let
J_e^Q(h) be the unnormalised mass of a COMPLETE joint public report event e.
For every labelled legal target t define D_e(t)=sum_h J_e^Q(h) 1[t is wolf].
The public decision face B_e is argmax_t D_e(t), with all exact ties retained.
Division by the positive event mass does not change that face. Let E_e(t) be
the same payoff moment under the actual private P event mass (including the
common Q-to-P likelihood transfer on its supported projection). The contribution
is sum_{t in B_e} E_e(t)/|B_e|. AFTER choosing B_e, events with identical labelled
faces may be summed. There are at most seven nonempty faces for three targets.
This is a scalar query reduction; it does not reconstruct a report posterior,
observed Board, Table, named history, or owner knowledge.

The development implementation receives already complete raw moments; it does
not yet replace the production publication generator or avoid its enumeration.
Tests reconstruct normalized posteriors independently and compare nine views
(public, true seer and wolf on each of three small conditional frames). These
frames use uniform earlier action prefixes. They establish the local final-day
identity, NOT reachability under the initial DEFAULT MAX policy or full MAX
Forecast/Table equivalence. Medium-claim competition is excluded from those
three generated frames. Tests also preserve zero-private-mass public events.

### Counterexamples that block broader averaging

A two-event exact example has expected report-conditioned maximum1 versus
maximum of averaged scores1/2. A private view assigning probability1 to target2
still follows a public policy selecting target1, giving0 rather than private1.
An abstract finite medium frame has two complete instructions tied at public2/3:
each separately evaluated private continuation is1/2, but mixing their public
observations FIRST gives private0. This is an algebraic counterexample, not a
claimed generated DEFAULT game. It fixes the instruction-selection/mixing order:
score whole candidates on Q, choose all minimising instruction IDs uniformly,
merge their chance outcomes, THEN condition Q and apply downstream report laws
and day MAX. In particular fake-seer plausibility must use that merged Q.

Another abstract timing counter has two seers with one redundant and two
informative next draws each. Choosing a bite before those draws gives5/6;
choosing after seeing both draws illegally gives13/18. The test is a finite
information-order counter, not an ordinary-engine integration verification.
No future acquisition or another player's secret may enter the wolf information
set used to choose the current bite.

## 2026-10-19 terminal MAX moment integration (opt-in)

The existing `grouped=True, aggregate_reports=True` closed consumer now admits
all-three-MAX policies in the same fixed-claim H0 scope. The default constructor
continues to refuse MAX. No policy field, runtime limit, API default, CO rule,
server or ordinary UI flow changes. The uniform path remains intact. MAX uses
ordinary named action/wolf cells and named publications rather than borrowing
the uniform target/report orbit reductions or terminal constant cache key.

### Production formula and preserved decision order

`CompiledPublications.terminal_execution_value` traverses complete named report
plans against the SAME indexed joint truth/fake kernel used by `event()`. For
each event it accumulates raw public wolf-target moments D and private moments E,
chooses the exact public argmax/ties via common `optimal_targets`, and adds the
uniform-tie dot product with E. Division by public event mass cancels in argmax.
It streams that scalar sum directly: there is no need to store seven face buckets,
construct normalized event Hypotheses/TrueScanState/Board, or solve forced final
executions. It enumerates complete candidate report plans, so this is not a
factorisation into reporter marginals or a claim of avoiding all enumeration.

`observer_projection_ratios` supplies the same roles/acquisitions likelihood
projection as the existing Q-to-P report transfer. It verifies private support;
the moment operation checks complete public/private mass. Initial private
guard/attack distinctions remain in the owned source; this terminal payoff
and report likelihood do not depend on their differing conditional records.
No acquisition records are removed or histories merged in any stored frame.

`InformationSetForecast.report_outcomes` extracts the unchanged common medium
instruction choice from `generate_morning`. Whole candidates are scored on Q,
all minimizing instruction IDs keep their uniform tie weights, and their
outcomes are merged BEFORE the final Q-conditioned seer kernel. The moment
consumer supplies only the equivalent final-execution scoring callback. It
still scores singleton instructions; singleton comparison removal is not part
of this change. Every selected positive-Q medium family compiles before P
transfer, including families with zero viewer probability.

Eligibility is unobserved scalar evaluation after acquisition and physical
projection: day, three alive, exactly one live wolf in public support, no initial
hunter, all disclosures, no scan retirement, no provenance instrumentation or
trace. Other frames retain their existing report/solver path. Observed reports,
Tables and history use complete ordinary named branches; warmed moment indices
can serve that same generator without substituting scalar moments for state.
Future personal observations are applied by the existing continuation context;
no future draw or another owner's secret is introduced into action ranking.

engines, not the moment emitter. Four generated five-player configurations
(single claim, true/fake pair, multiple true seers plus fake, competing mediums)

scalar, complete Forecast, public/physical Tables, all emitted report children,
observed child Forecasts/Tables, unchanged source, warm cache repeat and cold
exported source reconstruction. Twelve initial views also compare complete
configured day/night/report MAX Forecasts and Tables against ordinary engines.

An explicitly supplied finite medium frame independently reproduces the tied
instruction example: both public candidates2/3, private actual value0 after

That frame is a kernel-order counterexample, not a generated full-disclosure
DEFAULT history. Further tests cover valid/invalid future owner scans, private

cancellation/work stops, nonterminal/diagnostic fallback and default opt-in gate.
The independent algebraic/development oracle tests from bdb8e93 remain separate.

breakdown of847819d's GroupedForecast MAX consumer: its full-renaming/colour

benefit; its~7.3s colour builder is absent on the current opt-in path. The saved
847819d bounded seven result records a stop, not a CPU/expansion breakdown.
Therefore no globally largest present bottleneck can be inferred from those
old percentages. We did not repeat the unchanged whole request to guess one.

Instead, small exact conditional frames test a specific repeated dimension:
obtained results of a true seer who died before publishing them. The two-seer
six-player origin, forced execute4/bite1 and observed surviving seer2 white1,
has12 Q histories (three wolf placements times four dead-seer last draws).
The next behavior-relevant Q contains3 histories. A known-wolf P reduces4->1;
a dead-seer selected observer who knows black3 must keep its actual result.

### Mathematical sufficient state and exactness conditions

For a scalar query let A be current survivors plus the owner referenced by any
selected PersonalInformation/SeerObserver. Write a hidden state as (x,z), where
x retains the COMPLETE role tuple, acquisitions of owners in A, every guard and
attack record, rosters, initial rule and observed-prefix metadata; z is only
acquisitions owned by players outside A. Under fixed claims, no resurrection,
H0 and the existing configured rules, future acquisition targets depend on
living owners' own histories and the ORIGINAL target roster (corpses included).
Future truthful publication and plausible-fake support inspect living reporters'
own records; medium truth and physical casualties depend on retained roles.
Wolf information retains its complete initial team and own attacks. No future
transition, legal action or observation reads z. All public past reports remain
in the separate unchanged past/Board, including reports by later-dead players.

Define Qbar(x)=sum_z Q(x,z), Pbar(x)=sum_z P(x,z), separately. The common chance
kernels commute with these sums because they depend only on x and the complete
retained public context. In particular every fake-support witness survives as
an x witness and no new living-owner witness is invented. For each named action,
public Q continuation values agree by induction. Exact argmax/argmin and ALL
ties therefore agree; evaluating that SAME selected policy on Pbar then gives
the original P value. This does NOT exchange expectation and max, condition Q on
viewer evidence, discard role correlation, or optimise independently per world.
The public-medium candidate choice still scores whole instructions and merges
selected outcomes before computing the seer kernel.

A selected dead observer can still require an obtained scan during subsequent
acquisition filtering. Its actor is conservatively included in A, even when it
supplies only a role or a future target. Blind deletion raises false
seer_view_scan_outside_public_support on the independent small counterexample.
A living seer's unvisited set changes if its own records are dropped; another
negative test fixes that distinction. The dead observer's actual Table has
named column3=1 but public-strategy scalar1/3, so replacing public strategy by a
private optimum would also fail. Unknown/dead target identities remain readable.

The quotient is defined only for coherent Q/P input. A private acquisition not
in original Q must remain outside support even if deleting z would hide the
mismatch. The shared observer_projection_ratios check runs on the ORIGINAL
full likelihood projection whenever scalar projection actually changes either
law. No unsupported private record is repaired or uniformly filled. This is
zero-support handling, not an owner/frame certificate or issued ledger.

### Adopted opt-in implementation and cache boundary

The common `project_inactive_acquisitions` operation sums complete immutable
Hypotheses with the existing normalizer. GroupedForecast applies it to Q and P
at solve/forced-action SCALAR boundaries only, for MAX aggregate_reports opt-in,
H0, no scan retirement, no provenance/trace and supported typed observers.
Other paths retain existing behavior. Each calculation owns a derived-state
cache keyed by the FULL input state and retained owner tuple; identity results
are not cached. The operation depends only on those arguments, so Board/rules/
past need not be duplicated in that derived-state key. ALL actual scalar/action
keys still contain their unchanged Board, public past, Q/P, observer, target and
physical context. Thus different decision/observation meanings are not aliased.
Cancellation/time is polled before reuse. No source Board/node, observed report
branch, saved history, owner Table or cold export is replaced by the quotient.

The failed public-only candidate is retained in DEVELOPMENT code/evidence:
projecting just pure-Q scalar calls leaves original and quotient P calls in
separate caches. In the actual prefix it doubled action/value expansions
230/50 ->450/100, increased17689->22264work and1.56->2.24s. State count reduction
alone was not grounds to adopt that candidate. Aligning BOTH laws restores
230/50 expansions and reduces repeated per-branch hypothesis work.

Because every terminal call uses its kernel's same named alive targets,6029

report faces. This is a lower bound on repeated calls, not a measured saved CPU

## 2026-10-19 Q-owned terminal moment reuse, bounded within one calculation

### Public key, private application and unchanged mathematics

The79564c7 development formula is now implemented in the common publication
kernel. For complete simultaneous seer report event e, Q first fixes the exact
public maximum execution face B_e with unchanged uniform ties. With likelihood
fiber x=(roles,acquisitions), retain the PUBLIC numerator
M_Q(x)=sum_e J_e^Q(x) r(x,B_e). Every request returns
sum_x [P(x)/Q(x)] M_Q(x) using the existing observer_projection_ratios operation.
The numerator is unnormalised, not a private value, posterior or independently
optimised per-world payoff. Scores and B_e are constructed before any P enters.
Private guard/attack distinctions have the same terminal execution payoff and
report likelihood at this existing three-alive/one-live-wolf/H0 boundary; their
original records and physical branch mass remain outside this scalar vector.

`terminal_execution_moments` uses the same indexed joint truth/fake kernel as
named event generation, preserving reporter correlations and plausibility.
`terminal_execution_value` validates the current private support/normalization
on EVERY request, including a cache hit, before applying its ratios. Supported
fibers with zero M remain valid; an unknown private fiber still refuses instead
of being mapped to zero or uniformly filled. Private support does not have to
be re-enumerated per public event: each common generated report kernel is
row-stochastic on every original public fiber, so summing after P/Q transfer is
exactly this dot product. Independent Cartesian/event checks remain in tests.
P application is charged by vector length even on a hit and polls the same
cancellation/time/work budgets. No probability tolerance or numerical cap changes.

The cache key is `(compiled_kernel_object, exact_named_target_tuple)` within the
owning calculation. This is an object reference with identity equality, not a
lossy numeric ID/hash or an issued certificate. The kernel owns the complete
immutable Q joint source, rosters/acquisition metadata, public past, alive/claim
labels and already configured fake-policy chance table; its factory is cached
under full Board/Q/past and a calculation-owned fixed Strategy. Thus changed
public Q weights, history, source/report correlations, labelled actions or fake
policy cannot reuse another kernel's numerator. Exact MAX/uniform-tie behavior
is fixed by this operation. Medium instruction selection and outcome merging
still happen FIRST in the existing shared flow; each resulting Q law gets its
own compiled seer kernel. Viewer P/role/scan evidence never generates this key
or vector. Dead-observer protection and Q/P scalar projection remain as79564c7.

Different calculations never share this cache, including cold reconstruction,
changed-input snapshots and equal-looking sources. Public Board, private Table,
named report/history/cache inputs and cold recipes remain full ordinary flows.
The existing MAX opt-in/terminal/disclosure/guardian/trace/provenance admission
boundaries and all application defaults remain unchanged.

### Memory and interrupted construction

A calculation owns one `PublicMomentCache`, an LRU capped at128 complete vectors
AND4096 retained fiber rows in total. Each stored vector is an immutable tuple
of immutable fiber/Fraction pairs. A result larger than the row capacity is
computed without retention; eviction or no-cache operation never changes value
or rejects a previously admitted query. A separate entry cap also bounds keys
for zero-length vectors. A vector is installed only after the whole exact
build and normalization check succeeds; exceptions/time/work stops leave no
partial vector. Other completed cached entries remain valid. No repeated
owner/frame authentication, proof tickets or global cache were introduced.

The bounds cover this ADDITIONAL retained cache, not all solver memory or a
hard byte reservation. Building an uncached vector requires memory proportional
to its input support. Existing report/source/action caches and20ms sampled
384MiB private/working-set stop,250000work/30s calculation cap and35s watchdog
remain unchanged in measurement. Capacity constants are cache policy, not new
gameplay/work/admission limits, settings or user-facing options.

ordinary InformationSetForecast values on public/seer/wolf P views; reverse and

Forecast/Table order also matches independent ordinary engines and cold source

A noncollision test has two Q priors with identical public scalar2/3 but swapped

No result is incorrectly reused. A supported private fiber with zero public

nothing. Warm-hit cancellation, expired time and an ALREADY exhausted work

repeat at least two enumeration ticks; it now tests genuine exhaustion rather

forbidden during the scalar query while observed history/Table generation later
uses the same ordinary complete branches.

The saved527136d initial-seven request stopped, so its counts describe a partial

The structural evaluation is a configured-policy DAG, not one final vote:
initial day public scoring and selection -> observed physical continuations ->

scans -> common acquisition/casualty/public Q update -> whole medium instructions

requests every named forced-action column and by-dawn value. Memoization shares
identical contexts, but a target or roster relabelling can leave an isomorphic
problem under a DIFFERENT complete named Board/history/source key.

### Chosen larger candidate: full-context configured-policy equivariance

Use the EXISTING constructed partitions, not marginally similar player classes.
For a frame F, a permissible permutation g must preserve its complete public Q,
actual P, named public past (including full simultaneous report meanings), public
and physical Boards, rosters, selected observer and known actor information.
Rules and configured symbolic policies must be label-equivariant. Common legal
actions and transitions must satisfy A(gF)=gA(F) and K(gF,ge)=K(F,e).

Induction on the finite game horizon then proves public action scores transform
under g. Exact argmax/argmin sets transform with them, and uniform weights over
ALL tied named actions commute with permutation. The SAME public-selected policy
evaluated on transformed P therefore gives the same scalar. No max/expectation
exchange, optimal CO/report rule, new interacting-max equilibrium or per-world
choice is introduced: this is equivariance of the EXISTING configured algorithm.

For a day max face T, sum over action orbits O using |O|/|T|, not equal weights
over orbit representatives. A finite private-payoff counterexample gives1/3
versus the erroneous3/8 when orbit sizes1 and2 are ignored. Public claimants can
be equivalent while a seer-private view has named columns0 and1; its public
policy value is1/2, not its private optimum1. Its owner/record targets must refine
the group BEFORE reusing an action representative.

For wolf-information orbit C, compute one representative's continuation using
Q conditioned on that COMPLETE known initial roster/own attacks, then multiply
by the SUM of actual P cell masses in C. Do not pool information sets before
minimisation. The independent two-information/two-action counterexample has
E[min]=0 but min(E)=1/2. Actor scope splits group blocks by roster membership
and fixes owned attack targets. Q remains public; no other actor's new secret
is supplied to the wolf before choosing a bite.

Existing common target tests already reuse SOME latent targets. Structural-only
analysis of the actual initial input followed by forced execute3 (no scalar,
Forecast or MAX scoring calls allowed) has6 named wolf-team cells, one constructed
team orbit, four legal bites per team but already3 existing common score
representatives per team. The incremental cold policy-scoring structure is18->3,
not the misleading raw24->3 as a claimed new gain. This is one six-night input,
not a global sixfold speedup or completion prediction. Likewise initial public
day scoring already exploits latent symmetry:7 raw labels need5 existing score
representatives, and constructed groups suggest4. Larger benefit also comes from
weighted evaluation of a tied latent face and complete named Table/dawn columns,
whose scalar expansions currently repeat several isomorphic named problems.

The development candidate reuses common act/scoring, actor_scope,
wolf_information_orbits, group fix/report refinement and existing chance flows.
It leaves MAX joint-report orbit aggregation OFF and leaves Q-moment cache/
physical/named observation generators unchanged. There is no new key alias,

## 2026-10-19: common entry refinement and bounded MAX opt-in

This checkpoint closes the two b5c438f entry gaps through common metadata
refinement, not full-law authentication in production. `_frame_groups` is used
by every generated Closed node and Grouped `solve`, `act`, and `wolf_policy`.
The scalar entry therefore covers report candidate scoring; wolf policy covers
Horizon's direct named execution. The named action target is fixed before
continuation evaluation. Initialization, report children, owner children, and
cold reconstruction all use the same constructor refinement.

### Construction invariant and equivalence

Groups are subgroups of the immutable calculation's initial partition. Each
entry only splits a parent class; it never merges classes. Fix public history
targets, dead positions, incoming report targets, the named intervention target,
and the observer's actor/known acquisition targets/future target. A wolf owner's
known initial roster and own attack targets are fixed by the common information
position operation before the existing Q-conditioned policy scorer.

Split remaining actors by their complete sorted outgoing public meanings:
seer acquired day AND published day, medium report day, opportunity/resolved
status, named target, and color. Equal simultaneous literal meanings retain a
class; a one-sided record, different day/color/target, or incoming observation
splits it. This is deliberately conservative: mutually exchanged target graphs
are not newly admitted. Parent scopes restore their own partition on return;
this does not merge evidence inside a child frame.

Under fixed CO, H0, common configured policies and generated Q/P updates, every
remaining transposition preserves public context, each owner's information,
legal actions and physical branches. Thus action continuation values are
constant on each target orbit, and equivalent wolf information cells can share
the common scorer while their masses are summed. MAX chooses among values of
the configured continuation policy; it does not invent optimal CO/report rules.
Public Q weighting, within-world secret history, and physical P remain distinct.
Paired scalar projection preserves the selected observer's known records; its
retained positions are invariant under these fixed groups. Full named Board,
Q/P, past, observer and target keys remain unchanged. No lossy marginal key,
issued proof ticket, production permutation scan, or case evaluator is added.

| morning reports and candidate scalar | common report metadata; all 49 actual-input report frames invariant |

| wolf policy and Horizon | public frame plus roster/own attacks, before Q-conditioned scoring |

| guardian/GJ, self-bite or unsupported history/policy/CO | no promotion of these entries |
| unknown observer / diagnostic provenance / trace / scan retirement | grouping disabled or existing rejection retained |

aggregate_wolf=True and aggregate_reports=True, all configured selectors MAX,
fixed CO/H0/all disclosure, and no self-bite, trace/provenance or scan retirement.
Defaults and factories are unchanged. The prior reports-only MAX route keeps its
named-target behavior. MAX joint report-vector orbit reduction remains disabled.
Named live interventions and their ActionEvidence cold recipes are distinct
from unsupported MAX observed saved-action replay. No guardian/GJ claim is made.
