### Formula and proof boundary

Let X include the full public history, Q, branch P, physical state and observer.
For the proposed report R, public exposure can establish
K(R=r|h,X)=k(r|X) on every positive-Q full hidden history h. This gives
Q_r(h)=Q(h) and P_r(h)=P(h) when the same channel applies to P and all support
checks succeed. It does NOT imply V(X,r) is independent of r.

The scalar is S(X)=sum_r k(r|X)V(X,r). Replacing the whole channel by one
continuation requires a sufficient retained state T: legal actions, configured
policy (including MAX tie distributions), physical payoffs and all future
information-conditioned transition kernels must factor through T, preserving
selected-owner records. For a shared target action vector it requires the
same values for every action, not merely equal maxima. Induction can then prove
V(X,r)=v(T(X)) for every supported r; only then does S(X)=v(T(X)).

Counterexamples in development tests distinguish what this claim does and does
not cover:

* An independent fair white/black public signal can select a history-configured
  wait/finish policy with payoffs0/1. Its expectation1/2 differs from retaining
  the white row0. This is a mathematical counterexample, not a new supported
  game policy.
* Public-history-dependent action scores(1,0) and(0,1) give E[max]=1 while
  max(E[scores])=1/2. Independence of the hidden prior does not remove history
  from the Bellman decision problem.
* The ACTUAL approved fake_uniform_options uses own previously reported targets.
  After initial fake2:black3, future options include target4; after publishing
  fake2:white4, target4 is excluded while unvisited targets remain. Both kernels
  are normalized and role-independent, but their future report distributions
  differ. Deleting or replacing that public record changes a real transition.
  This is a transition counterexample, not a demonstrated numerical difference
  for a correctly proved stronger quotient under the current fixed policies.
* An actual still-possibly-true seer report changes full Q and terminal MAX
  values across children. Its observations cannot be treated as known-fake noise.
* Missing the original declaration disables existing retirement validation;
  selected-owner input and nonterminal input are excluded from the experiment.

Thus the proposed simple nonterminal suppression fails its sufficient-history
contract. A more powerful quotient might sum successive noise channels while
retaining history sufficient statistics, but this experiment does not establish
that theorem, count its eligible cases, or justify connecting it to production.
Do not flip the old retirement flag: its continuation_context drops past and its
role projection is not a proof of preserving every observer/private-history field.

### Safe terminal boundary and measured benefit

A small, COMMON-GENERATED source starts with5 players, dual seer claims,
initial1:white3 and2:black3 under random-white initial rules. Execute1 and
bite3 through common named generators;3 survivors remain and2 is publicly
exposed as fake. All next executions terminate, no medium report is due, and
no selected observer is present. Enumerating all fake reports leaves COMPLETE
Q/P posteriors unchanged (past changes), so each current action score and MAX
policy are invariant; no later legal/history policy remains after execution.

Independent fully named report enumeration, existing moments and a
DEVELOPMENT-ONLY terminal skip all give value1, with work38 /21 /3. Full current
Forecast/Table, named report rows, source immutability and fresh cold source
also agree. This gain is18work relative to the existing terminal path, in an
already certain-win terminal example. It supplies no nontrivial five-alive
continuation saving and no bound on the seven backlog.

Decision: do NOT adopt the nonterminal candidate or connect the tiny terminal
experiment to production. The proven boundary is narrower than the proposed
branch-family reduction; a special terminal case for this limited gain would
not solve the identified five-day/four-night cost. Production remains the
existing common evaluator/moment formula, with no added assurance scans,
ledgers, new evaluator, limit/default/publication change. Any future candidate
must retain/report the actual seen-target and owner/history dependencies and
prove the common full-history quotient before an opt-in connection.

Seven development tests pass in0.084s. `exposed-fake-channel-results.json`
retains every root event and report attribution;
`exposed-fake-channel-validation.json` embeds the test log, exact sum checks,
hashes and production-unchanged verification. No broad production regression
rerun is claimed because production was not edited.


## 2026-10-19: report-history sufficient statistics, calculation-key experiment

The earlier nonterminal report DELETION remains rejected. This experiment keeps
all generated report rows, complete public history, immutable named sources,
exports, replay and rendering. Only DEVELOPMENT scalar result cache keys use a
summary. No production method or configuration was changed; all production
hashes still match the490df94 measured source.

### Actual current consumers: history inventory

| common operation | fields actually needed; what cannot be inferred from a target set |
| --- | --- |
| seer_fake_uniform.fake_uniform_options | original roster, actor, set of that actor's previously PUBLIC reported targets; past colors/order/days do not enter this function |
| morning_publications.fake_backlog_options | actor/acquired-day publication set, rosters and current day, initial_rule; a target set does not say which acquisitions remain unpublished |
| plausible_fake_options | full public-Q truth support and possible unpublished (acquired day,target,color) acquisition signatures, in addition to the above; publicly exposed fake retains the uniform fallback |
| seer_future_uniform.true_reports | complete real acquisitions with target/color/day plus published actor/day set; initial and delayed publications cannot be synthesized from role marginals |
| seer_future_uniform.scan/acquire | each TRUE owner's PRIVATE visited acquisition targets and acquisition roster; public fake target sets cannot replace these |
| report_publication / initial conditioning / saved observations | acquired/published timing, target,color, legal current batch, initial fake declaration, full observed evidence; not covered by a continuation cache key |
| information_set_forecast day/night/medium MAX | full public Q and Q-conditioned owner information for scoring; branch P, observer, physical outcome for integration; report colors may already be encoded in Q/P |
| information_weights push / ratios | complete (roles,acquisitions) joint fibers and private guard/attack records; equal role marginals are insufficient |
| group_partition._public_groups | outgoing acquired/published day,target,color and medium opportunity/resolved fields, incoming target; partition must not be silently broadened by a smaller key |
| public / wolf action history and legal targets | named executions/deaths, full wolf roster and own attacks; guardian histories excluded from this candidate |
| CompiledPublications / named branches | source-owned past and full new report vectors, truth/fake kernel, complete Q fibers; sharing the object would return another source's history |

Thus the seen-target set is sufficient for ONE fake-target selection operation,
not for the calculation state. Seen-acquired-day set is needed for backlog,
other reporters' true records and full Q/P must remain, and publication timing
still belongs to validation/export even if one continuation operation ignores it.

### Narrow proposed key and transition argument

Development eligibility: on full positive-Q support, the live seer-CO false set
is the SAME singleton {f} in every hypothesis. Every other living seer CO is
therefore true in every hypothesis. This is stronger than matching truth/role
marginals and avoids inventing multi-fake correlation. Fixed CO and common
generated H0 frames, all configured selectors MAX, existing explicit full
aggregation opt-in, no trace/provenance/retirement/self-bite are retained.

For eligible valid frames define T_f(past) as:

* actor f and ALL of f's original acquired-day1 declaration records;
* union of f's publicly reported target labels;
* set of f's published acquired days;
* ALL other actors' complete report records.

The experimental scalar key is (method,Board,complete Q,T_f,complete P,
observer,physical,optional named target,refined constructed groups). Rules and
configured strategy are fixed within the calculation owner. Only f's later
colors, ordering, target/day pairing and publication-day decoration are absent
from this key. They remain in every actual source. The physical/action history,
initial declaration, true secret results and full Q/P fibers are never projected.

At the current fake law, a next report is sampled from unvisited public targets
(or all legal targets if exhausted) and uniform colors. Given T_f, the same
pending acquired-day set and roster produce the same chance kernel. Appending
new reports updates target/day sets by union. True reports are generated from
retained Q/acquisition records and other actors' retained publication histories.
A known fake cannot become true by subsequent positive-Q conditioning. This
suggests a closed continuation summary for these exact rules, while the full
Board/history and Q/P carry all other legal-action and information dependencies.
It is not a license to drop colors that changed Q in an earlier observation.

Refined groups are included conservatively because the implementation reads
complete public meaning metadata; observed cache aliases in this experiment
are only fake-color pairs with identical groups. Cached values are scalars,
never a source-owned CompiledPublications or generated named branch object.
No full-law proof scan/ticket is proposed for production.

### Independent small evidence and actual effect

Common generated SIX-player input: dual claims,1:white3,2:black3, initial
random-white rule. Force execute3 then bite4; four players survive. Reports are
still fully generated.32 rows form16 two-row fake-color pairs with equal full
Q/P and equal summary. Across public, true-seer owner and wolf owner views,
96 complete independent named Forecast/Table/cold reconstructions match within
48 two-row buckets; each named full history remains distinct.

Same shared250000work/30s per small request, no seven root/subproblem or sharding:

| complete small request | original work | development summary-key work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| five single MAX Forecast |512|512|
| five dual MAX Forecast |676|676|
| five three MAX Forecast |3538|3538|
| five medium-pair MAX Forecast |1153|1153|
| independent six-origin four-alive morning scalar |463|275|

The six-frame experiment has16 scalar aliases/hits,140 full eligible keys ->124
summary keys. Work decomposition is report84->84, acquisition3->3,
evaluation376->188. This is a40.6% LOCAL total reduction in a certain-win known-
wolf example, not a seven speedup. The report rows and their generation are
unchanged. Full public/owner named reference equality does not prove all future
entry or observer conditions globally.

Five-input report-heavy total remains5879work in BOTH modes. Three inputs have
no eligible scalar key calls; dual has7 eligible calls but7 distinct full keys
and zero aliases. Those five-player paths reach the terminal report moment
formula before a second nonterminal color-history family accumulates. The
4913/5879=83.57% report-attributed work is mainly inside generation/kernel/moment
operations; shrinking an outer scalar history key does not remove their internal
plan products. The already implemented moments still enumerate complete
truth/fake signatures as needed to select the public-Q optimal terminal action.
The present result provides no large-saving estimate for seven MAX.

### Boundaries, counterexamples and decision

Thirteen development tests (new6 + previous7) pass in0.493s. They check generated
color-pair equality, full named/cold outputs, exact named history preservation,
ambiguous fake and diagnostic exclusions, and differing Q/P secret fibers with
the SAME role marginals remaining distinct. Changing a true report color or the
fake's initial declaration remains distinct. An isolated common-backlog kernel
counterexample has the same seen targets but different published acquired-day
sets, producing a pending report versus none; it is explicitly a generator unit
input, not a claimed reachable whole-game trajectory.

NOT established: arbitrary observed/saved/edited/reordered/delayed report entry,
prospective/dead-owner combinations, guardian/GJ, dynamic CO, multiple fake
correlation, custom/history-sensitive future policies, or generalized initial
fake law. Their validation and complete history cannot reuse this smaller key.
Future rules that read old fake colors, report pairings or publication timing
would invalidate the summary and require a versioned new argument/proof.

Decision: retain this as DEVELOPMENT ONLY. A restricted scalar-key equivalence
has positive small evidence, but no broad entry contract and no gain in the
report-heavy five-player requests. It does not justify a production cache alias
or a promise to resolve seven's limit. The design limit is concrete: full Q/P
truth/secret correlations and pending-day/seen-target dependencies must remain;
key compression can share duplicate results after some report histories, while
it cannot by itself remove the costly complete observation kernel. Original
nonterminal deletion remains rejected; no publication/limit/default change.

Evidence: `report-history-summary-results.json`,
`report-history-summary-validation.json`, development fixture and tests. The
validation embeds logs, exact totals and measured-production hash checks.


## 2026-10-19: sparse positive-Q joint report prototype; not adopted

`benchmarks/player_match_groups/sparse_report_moments.py` is DEVELOPMENT ONLY.
Production and public defaults remain unchanged. No seven root or subproblem was
run. The candidate only changes development terminal moment construction, never
named event generation, full report/history carriers or Q/P filtering.

### Formula and algorithm

The compiler already owns complete full-Q hypothesis groups I[f,t] for each fake
identity set f and true-report plan t, plus the configured fake plan chance C_f(u).
For each positive supported (f,t,u), join disjoint reporter plans to a literal
complete public event e. Accumulate

    J_Q(e,x) = sum_(f,t,u compatible with e) sum_(h in I[f,t],fiber(h)=x) Q(h) C_f(u)
    A_Q(e) = argmax_a sum_x J_Q(e,x) reward(x,a)
    M_Q(x) = sum_e J_Q(e,x) mean_(a in A_Q(e)) reward(x,a)
    V_P = sum_x (P(x)/Q(x)) M_Q(x)

All contributions to the SAME observable e are added BEFORE A_Q(e), including
contributions from different latent fake identities. Full target/color/acquired/
published-day plans remain in e; complete (roles,acquisitions) fibers retain
correlations. Existing common ratio/support checks apply P afterward. Q-positive
P-zero events still determine public decisions and are never removed by a viewer.
Nonnegative mass means only events with zero JOINT Q mass can disappear; no
positive event, hypothesis correlation or uniform MAX tie mass is altered.

The prototype traverses the compiler's indexed truth plans x positive fake
chances, accumulates event/fiber dictionaries, and uses the EXISTING common
optimal_targets/tie rule. It does not choose an action separately per hypothesis
or invent a case evaluator. It charges every component join and hypothesis term,
polls cancellation, and checks complete Q mass. Unlike the current streaming
Cartesian loop, it also retains all positive event rows while building moments;
no memory-saving claim is made.

### Measured waste and cost

Four independent FIVE-player configurations x public/seer/wolf views, both
existing dense and sparse development modes:24 completed requests. Every request
keeps the same250000work/30s limit. Instrumented Forecast timing ends before
separate dense/sparse row diagnostics; diagnostics remain charged inside the
SAME original request budget and are reported separately. No ceiling reset or
unbudgeted continuation is used to finish a request.

Across the12 baseline requests' actual terminal moment builds:

| item | exact count |
| --- | --- |
| marginal Cartesian candidates |1643|
| positive complete public events |1547|
| discarded zero-Q candidates |96 (5.84%)|
| positive hidden truth/fake component plans |2036|
| positive hypothesis mass contributions |3017|

Only three-seer inputs have zero-Q Cartesian candidates:32 each in public/seer/
wolf requests. Single, dual and medium-pair inputs have ZERO such waste in the
measured moment builds. The current configured noncontradictory fake policy
already restricts marginal candidates; supported full vectors cannot be compared
to an imagined unrestricted independent color product.

Sparse iteration removes96 zero candidates but encounters489 extra positive
components that converge to the same event (2036-1547). These are not dispensable
zero branches: they are different supported hidden explanations. Net additional
charged work is489-96=393. Existing full support/correlation arithmetic remains.

| full initial MAX request | public dense->sparse | seer dense->sparse | wolf dense->sparse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| single |512->512|574->574|551->551|
| dual |676->703|817->844|541->568|
| three |3538->3612|4944->5072|4298->4408|
| medium-pair |1153->1153|1355->1355|1055->1055|

Total across12 requests:20014->20407work (+1.96%).

Single-sample instrumented Forecast times sum0.5214s dense /0.5159s sparse.
This tiny timing difference is not a repeatable speedup claim; work worsens and
intermediate event storage increases. No large measured benefit is established,
including on the report-heavy three-seer family. These totals are current
already-grouped production, not the earlier pre-group26067work baseline.

### Independent semantic comparisons and counterexample

Sparse event/fiber maps match the independent Cartesian diagnostic exactly on
every measured build. Separate existing expanded future_publications generation
matches full positive joint maps on four terminal fixtures.12 complete initial
public/personal Forecast/Table results match the fully named common-flow
reference with equivalence/renaming/value-law shortcuts disabled; immutable
sources, complete named reports and fresh cold reconstruction remain equal.

A normalized isolated common-kernel input with two possible fake identities and
the existing uniform fake policy creates the SAME white/white public event from
two worlds. Its public-Q optimal targets are(1,2), with equal tie weights. After
joint aggregation this event contributes half its mass as village payoff;
choosing the wolf separately inside each hidden component contributes all its
mass and is wrong. The same kernel has positive-Q events with zero mass in a
selected one-world P; sparse construction retains them and P uses the Q policy.
This kernel unit input is not claimed to be a reachable three-player full game.

Zero-payoff target subsets keep the existing empty moment-vector representation.
Moment construction does not call posterior-event generation. Five development
tests pass in3.692s, including cancellation and work-stop boundaries. The unit
fixture explicitly passes uniform fake policy to BOTH compiler and independent
reference; ordinary measured five-player requests retain their configured
noncontradictory-uniform policy.

Decision: NOT ADOPTED; no opt-in connection. The exact positive-Q meaning is
preserved, but eliminating zero candidate combinations is a small opportunity
in these actual inputs, while reorganizing positive latent contributions adds
work. Do not drop/independently optimize overlapping positive components to
manufacture savings. No seven retry, publication, upper-limit or default change.
Evidence: sparse-report-moment-results.json (raw measurement hashes retained;
final reference-helper policy parameter note included),
sparse-report-moment-validation.json, development fixture and5-test log.


## 2026-10-19: nonterminal full-horizon root-secret payoff vector; HOLD

User requirement: exact evaluation THROUGH TERMINATION; no approximation,
sampling, lookahead cutoff or changed policy. Permission is for a small
DEVELOPMENT prototype to decide whether to continue, not a product replacement.
Implemented payoff_vector_experiment.py and isolated payoff_vector_probe.py.
No production code, limit, default, public route or deployment changed.

### Scope and exact meaning

One immutable initial-five fixed-CO H0 input, no medium/hunter, supported all-MAX
strategy. Root basis i is the COMPLETE initial (roles,acquisitions,guards,attacks)
hypothesis, not a role marginal. The initial mixed law P0 is the public complete
Q. Common generation retains every initial record; descendant records identify
its root ancestor. At each generated frame a vector records conditional joint
terminal village payoff mass attributed to each ancestor i. Branch probabilities
are the SAME common physical/acquisition/report/actor policy probabilities;
normalizing a child P is canceled by its parent branch mass when summing.

The final root coordinate is

    u_i = E_Q[1(village wins at eventual termination) * 1(root=i)]
    v_i = u_i / Q(root=i)
    value(P) = sum_i P(root=i) v_i

The same vector is built for each forced current action. Public day choices use
existing Q day_scores/MAX/ties. Night choices use existing wolf_choices, which
conditions PUBLIC Q on the acting wolf's own roster/actions and weights the
actual P information groups. No viewer P selects a public or another owner's
action. All generation, legal resolution, Q/P update, report likelihood transfer
and initial owner filtering reuse the COMMON production operations. No fixed
number of plies ends evaluation: five-day and four-night lead to the exact
terminal-three formula, or common actual termination earlier.

The first prototype expanded all terminal report children; its measurements are
preserved in payoff-vector-expanded-terminal-results.json. It was unnecessarily
unfair to the production terminal moment optimization. The final prototype
reuses the EXISTING public-Q terminal moment/cache and scatters each full
P/Q-weighted secret fiber to its initial ancestor coordinate. Q-positive/P-zero
observations remain in Q policy construction. No new terminal evaluator or
report deletion is introduced. Full report/cold APIs keep their complete sources.

Reused views are current initial owner information (seer role/known results or
wolf roster), with P absolutely continuous on this exact root support. A future
SeerObserver acquisition intervention is NOT aliased to the same linear
operator: filtering/renormalization can depend on the chosen P and surviving
path. It explicitly retains the original exact scalar evaluation, including
original Pending behavior and owner-death cancellation semantics. This preserves
meaning, but gives no vector performance claim for future interventions.
Other Board histories/calculations, guardian/GJ, medium strategic reports and
changed policies are outside this small prototype, not newly supported. Horizon
outputs use the unchanged exact by-dawn evaluator; their cost is INCLUDED in
both scalar and vector measurements.

### Fair comparisons: current scalar reuse is already present

The baseline is current production with grouped/aggregate_wolf/aggregate_reports
explicitly enabled. Each request uses ONE calculation and shares its existing
Q-policy/moment caches between requested views. The vector request uses that same
Q strategy engine PLUS one actual-P vector continuation pass. It does not pretend
that public-Q ranking or terminal Q/P separation was missing from production.
Both modes share input preparation and owner selection before timing Forecasts.

Two independent five-player inputs: dual CO (root width2), and three seer CO
with two actual seers (root width3). Public-only requests have1 view. Multiple
requests contain public plus EVERY supported root seer-owner record and wolf
roster:5 views for dual and7 for three. These are natural supported views, not
repeated identical requests chosen to amortize preparation artificially.

Eight final FRESH workers complete within the EXISTING250000work/30s,
384MiB sampled/35s watchdog limits. No seven request is run. Single instrumented
samples are not timing distributions; reported peak memory includes imports,
setup and all requested views, not vector payload alone.

| input/views | scalar work ->vector | scalar seconds ->vector | peak working-set MiB scalar ->vector | peak private-commit MiB scalar ->vector |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| dual/public1 |676->1520|0.0361->0.0561|32.86->32.91|22.78->22.18|
| dual/public+owners5 |988->1588|0.0566->0.0840|32.75->33.14|22.05->23.24|
| three/public1 |3538->6151|0.0737->0.1439|32.84->33.31|23.06->23.12|
| three/public+owners7 |6152->6316|0.1867->0.1847|33.54->33.70|23.18->23.16|

The ~1% timing difference on the last row is noise-scale, with2.67% MORE work;
no total speedup is asserted. Public-only work increases2.25x (dual) and1.74x
(three). Multiple-view total work increases1.61x and1.027x respectively.

Incremental extra-view work DOES fall: dual312->68, three2614->165. But the
operator's initial cost is not recovered in either complete request. Cached
value/action vector counts stay6/23 for dual and6/29 for three across all views;
physical branches25/32 are traversed once. Width2/3, dense retained coefficients
INCLUDING distribution vectors are62/114 (nonzero40/57). Initial expanded-
terminal version retained938/2274 coefficients and was much slower; those are
not the final comparison. The final small basis says nothing about nine-player
coefficient widths or memory bounds, and process-memory differences this small
are not evidence of a memory improvement.

### Why this does not remove the single-public seven wall

The current public/acting-owner Q ranking computation still consumes the common
scalar evaluator; it already reuses Q policies and terminal moments. The prototype
adds another vector pass and retains extra acquisition/physical distributions.
Scalar target symmetry may also permute SECRET vector coordinates rather than
make two vectors equal, so literal action vectors cannot simply copy one scalar
representative. Replacing the Q ranking evaluator itself with local full-secret
transition operators would be a separate larger design with changing future
secret bases; it was NOT implemented or established here.

Consequently this experiment supports only cheap incremental P application after
an expensive operator is built. It does NOT show a route through single-view
seven MAX's existing limit. No nine-player runtime estimate follows, and no
seven benchmark is justified by these small results.

### Independent correctness and decision

All final scalar/vector worker pairs match complete Forecast (all forced named
actions and by-dawn outputs) and Table hashes. Five development tests pass
in13.767s: independent fully named common-flow Forecast/Table for public and
multiple owners, reversed repeated views without growing the vector tree,
every root-secret point P and a nonuniform P mixture, source/named-report history
immutability and cold reconstruction. Each conditional root payoff stays in[0,1].
A valid future scan and an already-visited unsupported scan retain their EXACT
original scalar result or Pending reason via separate fallback. Cancellation,
work limits and immutable calculation boundaries are still enforced.

Decision: HOLD; development-only, no large product replacement. Total work does
not improve even for natural multiple views; memory is not improved. Stop this
small trial here, with no additional seven run. Retain the current implementation
and previous unsupported boundaries. Evidence: payoff-vector-results.json,
payoff-vector-expanded-terminal-results.json, payoff-vector-validation.json,
prototype/probe and5-test log. No complete repository rerun is claimed because
production was not changed.


## 2026-10-20: local real-calculation GameTree, four citizens / one wolf

User-approved scope is inspection before further performance work. Added a
local-only --game-tree / GAME_TREE_PREVIEW flag (default false; hosted enable
rejected), using the existing GameTree panel and original GameRuntime cache.
No abilities, mutual strategic MAX, new role, approximation or horizon cutoff.
Existing benchmark safety limits and public default evaluation are unchanged.

CalculationTree owns one immutable BoardData snapshot and caches actual Board
records. The root stores the SAME RoleState.forecast result used in
BoardOverview. On expansion, existing action_outcomes generates each named
physical observation; the SAME exchangeable_forecast evaluator computes its
child value. Action values come from the actual completed Forecast, not a second
tree recurrence. Independent enumeration and parent/child Bellman consistency
checks live in DEVELOPMENT tests, not repeated solver authentication loops.

Important distinction: the ordinary evaluator already computes through terminal
states using symmetric count reduction. It has not expanded every named history.
The UI says "terminal value computed / named branches not expanded" until the
user unfolds that Board. It never pretends unvisited named histories were traced
by the compressed evaluator. Opening a Board performs real evaluation only if
its record is absent, then reuses it; existing count-state reuse remains intact.
This is a lazy view of actual evaluation and common physical transitions, not
a decorative independently computed probability tree.

Eligibility is five original players, one wolf, no medium/seer/hunter, latent
claims, self-bite forbidden, and a full uniform live single-wolf public prior.
Uniform support is checked at the external inspection boundary so historical
nonexchangeable evidence cannot enter a count-only inspector. The initial and
common generated continuing Boards preserve this symmetry. All public daytime
choices are permutation-equivalent, so existing MAX ties are uniform. At night,
the wolf knows its own location and selects uniformly among equally good LEGAL
nonwolf victims; the displayed target-selection probability is the public-Q
mixture, not the wolf's within-information-set probability. The UI explains
this distinction and separates MAX ties from the manual representative selection.
Conditional observation probability is conditional on that named chosen action.

/game-tree returns the actual Board, full action history, alive/dead locations,
initial-role wolf probabilities, configured rules/policy, exact fraction values,
viewpoint, candidates/selected ties and selection mass. It exposes computed
versus pending expansion and reused results. Only one node's actions are
serialized per expansion; nested Boards open on demand. A snapshot mismatch
returns409, unknown Board404, unsupported scope422. Existing request budgets and
calculation semaphore apply; a resource stop is JSON503 Pending without partial
success. Read-only inspection never advances or rewrites the saved game.
The cache is calculation-owned and bounded by existing per-game runtime eviction.

### 2026-10-20 Shared joint-action/outcome kernel (approved local migration)

Implemented in werewolf/joint_action_kernel.py and consumed by both configured
night scalar evaluation and GameTree. Actor information is conditioned on PUBLIC
Q before selecting a policy. The physical P measure supplies owner/action and
outcome masses. Established constructed player partitions, not equal values or
new permutation discovery, supply actor/victim orbits. Legal target orbits are
formed inside each actor stabilizer before resolving a representative action.
A joint plan carries its complete conditional source plus named owner/victim
transports; no representative wolf becomes a globally fixed world.

Each representative uses the existing resolve_action/acquire/report_children
kernels. For fixed reporters, newly observed target labels are assigned canonical
slots within the already constructed actor/victim stabilizer. Equivariance of the
common acquisition/death/report generators makes these outcome laws relabelings;
weighted images are retained and their full Q/P/public/private histories are
compared in development tests. Multiple exchangeable reporters conservatively
retain named report outcomes. Distinct black/white and same/different victim
relations are never merged. Scalar continuation evaluates each retained outcome
representative once. This is a stochastic action-group kernel, not a claim that
all its outcomes are the same state.

Configured action mass is separate from conditional outcome probability. A
positive group conditions on its configured policy; owner/action cells with
zero chosen probability do not acquire artificial mass. For a wholly zero-mass
group, the existing uniform-legal counterfactual measure defines displayed
conditional outcomes; it never contributes to the scalar value. Under MAX in
B4, candidate-group action mass is0 and its conditional black/white split is
1/2 each: both actual reach masses are0. This conditional split is recomputed
from the joint law, not hardcoded or assumed for asymmetric histories.

Current mapping uses named recipes. Visible representative nodes are generated
from the same kernel child source. Other actor/target/observation images remain
lazy until requested; Current restoration uses common named owner/action/report
generators and an exact report signature. Full exported cold replay and both
representative/nonrepresentative Current requests match. Existing integer report
selectors remain valid; negative/invalid selectors are rejected.

Scope: night, no hunters, no explicit observer or provenance/trace hook, all
seer/medium disclosure, full tracked living seer histories, and publicly certain
true-seer CO positions. Existing exact paths handle uncertain/latent/fake seers,
explicit private observers, unsupported MAX aggregation settings and other

### 2026-10-20 Private result factorization and attack stabilizers

The retained simultaneous measure now displays scan position -> private result
-> attack position -> morning Board. There is no intermediate solver decision,
new public evidence, or wolf-policy recomputation. Results come from the actual
current-night acquisition, even when the seer dies that night. Missing seers get
an explicit no-scan result; incomplete histories retain the earlier exact joint

night_abilities.seer_paths is the shared generative acquisition program. It emits
each true seer's target and immediate private result in ascending actor/table
order, then proceeds to the next seer. Own-unvisited filtering reads only that
actor's pre-night history. acquire_uniform, including ordinary scalar facades,
consumes this generator. Simultaneous eligibility always uses the night-start
alive roster. The existing complete grouped-report Closed mode now expands only
missing initial private records once, preserves their original Q/P world masses,
and retains complete histories through recursion and cold export. Ordinary
non-stage Closed/reference facades keep their prior reconstruction contracts.

night_program.compile_night commits wolf information-set policies from night-
start public Q before any private-result partition. It builds the exact joint
night measure through existing acquisition, casualty, projection and publication
kernels. NightStage holds explicit P-prefix states with frozen public Q and
unchanged later-actor information. Its conditional target/result/attack edges
factor this SAME law. GroupedForecast's scalar night continuation and GameTree
consume the same program and its prefix states. Death resolution stays after
acquisition; same-night seer death retains the result but preserves existing
publication eligibility.

Target orbits are generic full-measure bijections within constructed partitions.
One source automorphism must fix all earlier stage coordinates and transport
every named remaining path, full child Q/P, histories, physical Board, actual
policy mass and counterfactual mass. Results are never grouped by equal win rate
or probability. Terminal outcomes use the same prefix stabilizer; equivalent
full outcomes are recursively evaluated once. Every named image retains its
cold Current recipe and display subtree. Unknown signature shapes fail closed.
There are no fixed 4/5 IDs, one-seer restriction, sampled worlds, new strategy
mode, horizon cut, or approximate score in this kernel. Named night actions and
physical/publication leaves are still enumerated before quotient construction;
this is continuation compression, not elimination of all action enumeration.

### Per-Item immediate-stage expansion and descendant collapse (2026-10-21)
UI expansion is separate from exact calculation cache. New TreeExpansion maintains a Set keyed by stable full display paths (kind, canonical node when available, sibling index) and deduplicated per-node pending loads. Every Board/composition/ability/result/attack Item projects only its immediate children when opened. Collapsing deletes its own and all descendant path expansion entries, preserving unrelated sibling branches. Reopening reveals immediate children only. Initial display is the collapsed root Board; expansion state is local to the mounted page and resets on full navigation/reload. Calculation cache is retained; hidden Items do not mean missing/renormalized probability. API/scalar/night-stage kernels and Current reconstruction are unchanged.

Known composition metadata opens without fetching full Board choices; lazy composition choices are loaded through the existing API once per pending canonical node. UI open state is set before awaiting data; responses only refresh the cache/view and never set open state, so a closed ancestor cannot be reopened by a late response. Disconnected roots ignore late render/error updates. Keyboard buttons expose aria-expanded; leaves cannot toggle. Pointer movement beyond5px suppresses Item/Current click activation; background pan remains unchanged.

### Anonymous relational migration plan (approved 2026-10-21)
Baseline4632978. Preserve the named engine as a development oracle and exact fallback. Generate structural choices by existing binders or one fresh slot from a cell/role, never enumerate labelled injections or test swaps. Counts alone are not a state. Independent named enumeration belongs in tests. Admission is by supported semantics/provenance, never a size or search cutoff.

Explicitly report any adapter which still reads existing named prior hypotheses or falls back to named continuation/publication. Never claim source generation or MAX policy evaluation has become anonymous until it has. Compare complete joint event masses, not only scalar values.

Public appearance is not a continuation cache key. Existing automatic CO timing is not expanded by this migration.

Preserve one-child chains, recursive collapse, sibling independence, Current reconstruction, CO cells, page-wheel/Ctrl-wheel/drag and measured Footer layout. ### Count-native belief ownership and public posterior (2026-10-21, stages1/2)
New anonymous_belief owns immutable weighted Population atoms and separate Q/P laws. Initial role occupancy tables are generated from group capacities and role counts with multinomial weights, then initial acquisitions bind anonymous people directly. No named role assignments or permutation safety tests enter this owner. Public names become explicit pins only when coordinates are distinguished; equality, old directed acquisitions, wolf membership, life status and multiple actors remain in each atom. Refining public coordinates integrates the exact slot multiplicities rather than choosing role truth. Exact-equal constructed atoms combine by hashing; there is no payoff-based or discovered-swap equivalence.

Execution and night public posterior updates now operate on those laws. Q acquires results and conditions corpse/winner compatibility; P additionally uses the frozen night-start actor policy. Private prefix analysis selects anonymous path rows and keeps Q unchanged. Multiple-night tests preserve old records and exclude already dead targets while retaining simultaneous-night acquisitions. Counterfactual law stays separate for zero-policy branches. Count-native compositions are read from the owner. A1002-person initial prior has one relational atom and fewer than10 post-execution atoms; this is a structural owner test, not a full-game timing claim.

Independent crossing paths have a white under-stroke to distinguish a crossing from a junction; independent segments are no longer globally merged. Probabilities prefer free horizontal segments after the branch split near their own destinations, avoiding other labels, cards and wires. Panel wrapping is measured before the single geometry pass.

### Pending semantic boundary: daytime publication timeline

The requested Day-start Board -> chronological public updates -> execution cannot be implemented by moving the existing publication column. Native nightly leaves currently hold the post-publication `source`; `compile_anonymous` obtains the pre-publication `child` from `_project_roles`, then applies `engine.report_children`. Public convergence also groups post-publication sources. A true arrival Board requires retaining/mixing the pre-publication law, separately branching the morning publication kernel, and assigning Current to the appropriate post-update state. Merely inserting a Board before the old labels would show future reports in its Q/P.

Initial/temporally separated CO has a further model issue: `board_roles.with_claims(initial_group=True)` invokes `group_initial_board`, while `ClosedCalculation` constructs its origin from the final saved CO collection. BoardLog records single/simultaneous order but explicitly is not a new claim likelihood (`public_log_changes`). Concrete regression:1CO(1->3white) reconstructs P1-wolf Q=0,P=0; adding2CO(2->4white) reconstructs Q=1/2,P=1/2. A Bayesian update cannot restore zero-support worlds. Consequently the independently reconstructed prefix snapshots must not be linked as if they were conditional transitions of the current fixed-CO model.

Pending decision before the requested complete timeline: distinguish an unfinished declaration round (hidden true seers remain possible until the round closes), with an explicit CO timing/selection law; or present earlier BoardLog prefixes as retrospective input snapshots, without calling them conditional future transitions/probabilities. The latter is a different graph meaning. No new CO rule was silently invented. The requested column move/rename and initial/time-separated update chain are deliberately not shipped as a cosmetic rearrangement while this semantic choice is unresolved. Counterexample is covered in `test_current_fixed_CO_model_cannot_be_reused_as_a_sequential_Bayes_update`. Existing layout/updates remain intact.

### Daytime public updates: recorded revisions versus generated reports

Supersedes the earlier pending-model discussion: no CO timing law is needed for the requested presentation. Recorded BoardLog batches are ordered *input revisions*, not stochastic transitions. `tree_public_updates.input_timeline` reconstructs the initial empty input and each committed prefix, keeping simultaneous events in one Item/column and separate batches in separate columns. Legacy input without timestamps remains one batch. These display-only snapshots have no likelihood, no Current identity and no future-action policy. Their composition distributions reflect their own input prefix; they do not feed a posterior into the actual calculation. The last update retains the existing real root/Current and final saved Q/P. No new CO acceptance or likelihood rule was added.

For generated mornings, `public_convergence.observation_branches(...,publish=False)` retains the existing shared `_project_roles` arrival Q/P before `report_children`. The Tree holds these immutable arrival sources separately, mixes only equivalent public arrival states, and records each existing post-publication Board with its conditional mass (post reach divided by arrival reach). Arrival village value is the exact weighted future value of those updates. Newly generated reports are absent from arrival public history. The original night program, policy conditioning, post-publication sources, P/Q, public convergence/Current routes and scalar evaluation are unchanged. The display factors death -> daytime arrival -> probabilistic updates -> execution; it does not recalculate a wolf policy after a private result. Dead true seers add no new report under the existing policy.

Frontend has phase-specific column widths/counts, Day1's actual number of input updates, a daytime update column for future reports, and no old night-end publication column. Update Items retain the post-update Current button and inline analysis. Arrival/input snapshots open their embedded composition analysis without requesting a generated Current or duplicating a log application. Initial snapshots deliberately omit continuation values rather than falsely reading the final input's value. Snapshot ancestors remain transparent to required-Current auto expansion. Original semantic ordering, minimal row packing, orthogonal routing, intrinsic widths, page wheel/Ctrl zoom and drag pan are unchanged.

### Persistent navigation migration — phase1 foundation

Added `NavigationHistory` over immutable `SolverState` and immutable `Occurrence` records. An occurrence owns its parent, JSON-owned selector, exact selection weight and branch-local bijection; a repeated solver input can be referenced by multiple paths without replacing the first parent. Solver identity includes fixed rules, configured strategy and constructed groups as well as complete Q/P source. This avoids accidentally sharing different policies after editable-board integration. Manual-public edges require selection weight0; the history is not an evaluator and cannot alter the original solver expectation. Selectors are copied through canonical JSON, and all name maps are full validated bijections. `CalculationTree` owns this new history but existing UI/routes are intentionally unchanged until the cursor integration is verified.

Phase1 evidence:4 new tests cover reconvergence with different parent/path/name mapping, exact shared node cold reconstruction, manual zero-edge source value/old-child invariance, selector immutability, policy separation and mapping/inverse/composition. Together with existing publication/relation tests13pass. No preview restart or new persistent port in this phase. Remaining migration is cursor/ordinary Board integration, manual observation adapters and persistent root ownership, then concrete representative transports. Footer's existing `Progress.settled_village/settled_wolf` must travel with navigation occurrences, so moving to a continuing child cannot drop settled terminal mass; this is a discovered adapter requirement, not a gameplay rule change.


### Navigation controller and replay journal (development, not yet enabled in UI)

NavigationGame now owns a cursor over immutable solver states. Footer actions keep
settled probability separately from the continuing conditional Board; manual CO
and current-day reports condition Q and P independently through existing support
filters, append zero-selection-weight occurrences, and never modify source values.
Historical report edits and unsupported medium input remain explicit unsupported
operations rather than invented evidence or a reseeded prior.

NavigationStore writes only immutable root inputs and replay recipes atomically,
with revision checks and in-memory rollback on validation/persistence failure.
Fresh stores rebuild exact sources instead of importing posterior snapshots.
Representative selection constructs full bijections from population groups or
rooted public-relation forests. Linked CO reports move together; no independent
actor/target swaps, permutation search, or payoff equality test is used.

A possible conservative public roll certificate (not implemented): let C cover
all living wolves across the relevant public Q and physical P histories, m=|C|.
At day start n>=2m+1, or before a night with at most one death n>=2m+2, executing
one remaining C member each day preserves a clean outside-C majority until all
wolves die. This certifies a public strategy, not arbitrary random play. Applying
it to configured MAX also needs public-policy/support compatibility (including
private histories), or a policy-closed proof for every positive-policy action.
A scalar value1 certificate does not supply all forced-action values or the
complete MAX tie distribution; those must remain exact. Never set Board.winner
while wolves still live. A future enabled cutoff should carry a proof scope,
preserve saved/current/manual paths and fall back exactly outside that scope.

Madman and fox are not current ROLES and there is no ballot/PP model. Extending
C to all hostile candidates can support the same clean-majority argument only
under specified coordinated voting, tie, death and terminal rules. Fox must be
proved absent/dead in every relevant supported history, not merely one private
component. No such future role semantics were added.

### Analytic public roll cutoff — exact value only

New games expose 勝利確定の省略, default On, with execution MAX locked while
On. Off permits random execution. win_cutoff is a boolean Strategy field,
included in BoardData inputs, calculation ownership, solver identity, cold
navigation root/history and existing strategy cache keys. Missing legacy fields
mean Off; old fixed-policy documents are never silently rewritten. Changing a
fixed navigation policy requires a new game, as before. Missing-field legacy API
clients also preserve their original strategy; the new setup always submits it.

The certificate module does no recursion: C is the union of live wolf candidates
in public Q, m counts actual people (not groups), n counts survivors. Require
day n>=2m+1 or night n>=2m+2. Execute remaining C members: execution plus a
worst-case outside-C night death preserves n-2m>=1; inside-C deaths help. Every
wolf remains in C, so eventual elimination is forced. The current public MAX
policy has all living targets available and therefore attains value1. This does
not force a particular roll ordering or alter MAX uniform ties. Private scan,
report and wolf strategies need not cooperate with the witness.

Admitted named scope: citizen/seer/wolf, no self-bite rule, public observer,
positive full physical support contained in Q (roles, acquisitions, guard and
attack histories). Unsupported roles/rules or support disagreement fail closed
to unchanged exact evaluation. Count-native no-report evaluation applies the
same bound with atom-support containment and pins/residual cell membership;
private certainty never shrinks public C. No madman/fox/vote rules were added.

Only scalar value caches receive1. Complete action tables, dawn values, MAX
scores and tie policies still use their normal exact generators. Tree read does
not automatically expand a certified Board; frontend auto expansion stops there,
except ancestors needed to retain a recorded Current/manual path. No terminal
winner is invented and saved/manual continuation remains navigable. Explicit
routing/history restoration still uses normal action kernels. Public main uses a
value-only Forecast with an explicit pending-metric marker and empty action
values; 候補別の勝率を計算 requests the normal exact detail table. /navigation's
summary likewise avoids calculating unused detail tables. No partial action table
is cached as complete. Legacy/non-navigation detail consumers remain exact.

### Roll-pruning explanation marker

The visible option is now exactly 縄が足りたらロラを省略. A certificate-only
Tree summary (no expanded action table) carries a presentation marker with
縄数X狼位置数Y. Y counts the distinct live public-Q candidates, not orbits.
X=floor((n-1)/2) in day, floor((n-2)/2) in night. Under the admitted at-most-one
night-death rules, these budgets are exactly the integer version of the
certificate inequalities X>=Y. The roll invariant above guarantees enough
safe executions; X is available roll capacity, not a promise to keep playing
after an earlier real victory.

The marker is placed in the current day's next execution-position column, or
the following daytime execution-position column for a night certificate. It is
a terminal presentation item, with a probability-free explanatory connector,
no Current navigation, no real action/Board/history entry and no continuation
stub. It opens automatically without loading policies. Required Current/manual
paths remain independently visible. Off and already-expanded full Tree action
tables do not carry the marker. BoardTable's explicit detail computation remains
a separate request and does not pretend to expand a future Tree.

### Manual public input: preserve joint role priors (2026-10-22)

`manual_public_input.condition_input` now treats hand-entered public evidence as compatibility, not a sampled report event. For each Q/P law separately, let M(r) be the incoming joint role-assignment mass and T(r) the compatible private-history mass. Remove assignments with T(r)=0; multiply every compatible history by M(r)/T(r), then normalize across surviving assignments. Unequal prior masses and within-assignment history ratios survive. Returned support masses describe surviving role support, not a CO/report generation probability. Manual navigation edges remain zero selection weight; parent states and forecasts remain immutable.

Q-supported input outside all physical P support is explicitly unsupported, rather than fabricating physical histories or misreporting a public contradiction. Report timing/acquisition legality and old-publication immutability still apply. Sequential reports under the same claims equal the batched reveal; genuinely different CO event order can impose different existing CCO constraints. Existing role marginals already reflect prior deaths/actions: this update does not reconstruct a uniform initial prior or remove past automatic strategy likelihoods.

Automatic `morning_publications` generation and ex-ante action policies remain unchanged. The delayed CO example (execute1, bite2, claimant3: initial white4, next black5) keeps three compatible assignments equally weighted: true seer3=1/3. Its automatic generated-report posterior remains13/25, which is intentionally a different experiment. Correlated private histories remain available for subsequent exact evaluation.

Saved navigation records contain evidence and cold-replay through this adapter, so replayed manual descendants adopt the new semantics; their pre-input ancestors remain unchanged. Existing running preview18821 is not restarted: its per-process session signing key cannot be preserved by the launcher without handling signing credentials. Browser validation uses an ephemeral server, temporary journal directory and isolated Edge profile; no user Board reset, persistent port or cookie migration.

Added adversarial tests for equal role counts with different guard↔seer memory relations (next joint-target equality 2/9 vs1/9), zero-policy paths retaining counterfactual guard/attack correlations, manual alternative hunter CO after an unobserved guard success (zero selection edges, unchanged parent, separate normalized Q/P, named exact value and cold journal parity). Existing unsupported hunter MAX and automatic CO policy semantics remain unchanged.

### Bounded boundary improvement: joined exact Q/P materialization
`expand_laws` shares only assignment and role/history record construction for the SAME immutable anonymous Atom. Each requested law retains its own exact weights and independent normalization. Distinct guard/attack memories are distinct atoms; duplicate rows add; structurally equal complete laws may reuse immutable normalized tuples. No persistent/global cache, no posterior flattening, and no changed solver/strategy rule are introduced. `source` requests Q/P together; the single-law `expand` API delegates to the same generator.

Independent named enumeration checks n4/5/8/9, unequal masses (public wolf1=1/n, private wolf1=2/(n+1)), duplicate atom rows, and differing correlated guard/attempted-bite memories. Full affected regression102 passed in112.453s; isolated real-browser seer Tree/Footer/Current/inline distribution/desktop/narrow flow passed and counted19 actual joined-boundary requests (largest law96 named rows). Existing user browser/session and normal18821 process were not restarted for this overnight optimization.

Bounded5-run median measurements: n8 unequal Q/P assignment work672→336,22.66→19.58ms; same law21.76→12.59ms. n9 work1008→504,33.47→29.43ms unequal,33.00→19.15ms equal. Outputs matched the previous algorithm exactly. These are materialization timings, not full-game speedups; distinct Q/P still emit separately weighted named distributions. Reproduce: `PYTHONPATH=. python dev_previews/benchmark_materialization_join.py`.

### Remaining boundaries and deferred semantics
Initial SavedOrigin still enumerates named joint roles/acquisitions before the supported anonymous owner is attached. Raw unconstrained n9 counts (1wolf/2seer/1medium/1hunter/4citizen) are7560 role allocations and up to370440 first-white acquisition histories before public filtering. Full ownership migration is larger than this bounded change and was not attempted without new oracle coverage. Public Q guard-observation conditioning and publication, and named continuation/Current reconstruction remain exact adapters.

Hunter MAX: existing guard targeting is uniform (`guard_choices`), NOT a newly optimized guard policy. The unresolved dependency is primarily the optimizing wolf/public continuation policy: its observation likelihood determines future public support, whose values determine that same policy. `require_profile` only accepts a completed selected certificate; cyclic/incomplete/multiple profiles stay Pending. A future safe task is to isolate and solve the complete same-context dependency component, retaining original information sets and tie rules; changing Q semantics or selecting an arbitrary profile is not authorized.

Automatic DayX/blackX CO remains a gray editor preview (`static/strategy.js`), not serialized as a calculation policy. Connecting it needs jointly specified true/fake actor-visible disclosure opportunities and old-result publication, separate from deterministic recorded manual inputs. Neither deferred semantic change was implemented overnight.

In the known-seer fixture after execution2, seer1 already visited3; legal new targets are4/5, not corpse2.
